# Contributions

A ledger of who contributed what to the proof, its checking and its explanation. Each entry links to a
public record. Entries describe what the record shows; they are not endorsements or verdicts.
Missing or misattributed contributions: please open an issue.

Public records and links below were checked online through 2026-11-22 05:25 UTC (entries added on 2026-11-23 were checked at 07:13 UTC that day; the LUMI statement-review R2 entry at 09:23 UTC); exact download hashes and earlier snapshots are recorded in [`sources/SOURCES.md`](sources/SOURCES.md).

## Format

Each entry: **who** (name or handle) — **what** (one sentence) — **kind** — **status** — **record** (link, date).

Kinds: `proof` · `formalization` · `compilation/kernel check` · `review` · `exposition` ·
`errata/gap` · `fix` · `commentary` · `this book`.

## The proofs

- **Levent Alpöge with Claude** supplied A2’s trace realization, its denominator-clearing helper and the consumer proofs traced locally in Chapter 1. [Pinned source record](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-1-12214-trace-realization)

- **Levent Alpöge with Claude** — Archive A2 supplies the actual majorant consumers in the Dirichlet tail, theta lattice, exponential expansion, inner-product and Poisson arguments traced in §1.2.2.11. [Archive index](sources/SOURCES.md#1-the-lean-archive-primary-source) · [Chapter 1 §1.2.2.11](chapters/01-statement-and-fidelity.md#12211-nonnegative-extended-sums-and-finite-majorants)

- **Levent Alpöge (with Claude)** — Announced a claimed proof of the Riemann Hypothesis and of the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis for all Dirichlet $L$-functions in a four-page note and a 14-part Lean 4 archive (175,187 `.lean` files on Lean `v4.33.1` and Mathlib `v4.33.1`, joined sha256 `9631b4f0…49c7fc`), without releasing a human-readable proof manuscript. — `proof` · `formalization` — [Paper] / [Lean] Claimed formal proof; machine checks reported below; human mathematical review not completed [Open] — [remark.pdf](https://alpo.ge/remark.pdf), [lean.tar.gz.part01](https://alpo.ge/lean.tar.gz.part01)–[part14](https://alpo.ge/lean.tar.gz.part14) (2026-11-20)
- **Alessandro Maria Masullo** — Released a 940-page human-written manuscript draft, *The Music of the Primes* (sha256 `e039d3f6…97bf5`), proposing an adelic trace-formula proof of the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis for all unitary Hecke characters of all number fields, in which Chapter 13 (deducing GRH from Theorem 12.5.1.1) remains a two-page placeholder. — `proof` — [Paper] Unfinished draft undergoing major revision; gaps and disputes reported below, peer review not completed [Open] — [the-music-of-the-primes.pdf](https://math216.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/the-music-of-the-primes.pdf) via [Vakil's announcement](https://math216.wordpress.com/2026/11/20/hurried-announcement-alessandro-masullo-grh/) (2026-11-20)

- **Levent Alpöge with Claude** — Supplied the real embedding producer and the Hermitian descent tensor-norm consumer read in Chapter 5. [Archive record](sources/SOURCES.md#the-same-tensor-norm-witness-at-a-real-place-53118)

- **Harish-Chandra** established the discrete-series character and
  formal-degree theorem. **Michael Atiyah and Wilfried Schmid** gave the
  compact-dual-normalized formula and the lowest-type theorem via their
  geometric construction. **Levent Alpöge with Claude** supplied A2's
  unitary-group translation, integral bounds and the uses of the calibrated
  degree. [Source record](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-9-translation-and-the-formal-degree-constant)

- **Adolf Hurwitz, Friedrich Hirzebruch, Ian G. Macdonald, Alan Edelman,
  Anthony W. Knapp, and Michael Atiyah and Wilfried Schmid** established the
  unitary-group volume formula, complex QR/Iwasawa measure decompositions, and
  compact-dual Haar proportionality normalization. **Levent Alpöge with Claude**
  supplied A2's `HaarVolumes` coordinate-density chain, `CDHaar` witnesses, and
  formal-degree scale bridges. [Source record](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-9-compact-dual-coordinate-density-and-haar-normalization)

## Checking and formalization

### Compilation, axiom audits and kernel checks (Alpöge archive)

- **Lean and Mathlib communities (endorsed by the Lean FRO and the Mathlib Initiative)** — Published a joint official verification statement certifying that Mathlib's `RiemannHypothesis` statement is vetted, that dozens of independent builds reproduce the same exported proof, that both `comparator` and `con-leche --compare` confirm statement matching against the Mathlib-only challenge file, and that Lean's kernel, the formally verified checkers `con-leche` and `con-ron`, and third-party checkers accept the inert export. — `compilation/kernel check` · `commentary` — [Community] Official joint verification statement published 2026-11-21 13:55 UTC (human mathematical digestion noted as the next stage) — [Lean community blog](https://leanprover-community.github.io/blog/posts/riemann-hypothesis/), [Zulip #642485818](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642485818) (2026-11-21)
- **Andrew Sutherland** (with Claude Code) — Compiled all 175,187 modules with zero errors, checked that the five theorems in `src/Main.lean` depend only on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound` and that 8,322 Mathlib and 378 dependency `.olean` files match the official cache, published the 61.57 GB `lean4export` output, ran `leanchecker`, `nanoda`, `con-leche` (including `--compare`), `con-ron --compare` (`3f22699b`) and `lean4lean`, completed an independent no-network bubblewrap-sandboxed `lake build Main` whose exported 61,570,595,788-byte proof stream (`5ea74b91…244e`) matches the published export byte-for-byte (with 175,186 of 175,187 `.olean` files identical at the same build path and the sole 38-byte bound-variable race in `LocModel.olean` `BBtop.eq_1` isolated to Lean `#15498`), and combined the archive with `kimihiro64/Robin1984` to verify unconditional divisor-sum inequalities under `con-leche`, `con-ron --compare` and `nanoda`, alongside passing standalone `Robin1984` through both Comparator `v4.33.0` and `lake comparator` (`v4.36.0`, including `Lean paranoid kernel`) and publishing the fully elaborated Mathlib-only term `lagariasStmt`. — `compilation/kernel check` — [Community] Compile, sandboxed `lake build` + byte-identical 61.57 GB export, axiom audit, `leanchecker`, `nanoda`, `con-leche` (`master` and `--compare`), `con-ron --compare`, `lean4lean`, standalone `Robin1984` `lake comparator` (`v4.36.0`), and combined `Robin1984` `con-leche`/`con-ron`/`nanoda` checks passed; single-threaded Lean default/paranoid export kernels and end-to-end `lake comparator` (`v4.36.0`, statement and axiom stages passed, kernels running) on the 62 GB main and combined exports listed as running on the 2026-11-22 04:35 UTC page [Open] — [math.mit.edu/~drew/grh/](https://math.mit.edu/~drew/grh/), [Zulip #642277030](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642277030) (2026-11-20; ### Proof-checking tools and independent kernels (results summary)

- **`leanchecker` (Lean FRO)** — Official Lean 4 `.olean` kernel replay tool, which replayed all 175,187 compiled modules with zero rejections in Sutherland's and DeDeo's builds. Sutherland also used it for the passing single-threaded default and paranoid v4.36.0 replays of the 61.57 GB text export in Sutherland’s subsequent checks. — `compilation/kernel check` — [Community] Module-by-module replay passed (175,187/175,187); export-level default and paranoid runs in progress [Open] — [math.mit.edu/~drew/grh/](https://math.mit.edu/~drew/grh/), [proofsandreasons.io/rh-oleans.html](https://proofsandreasons.io/rh-oleans.html) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-21)
- **`nanoda` (`nanoda_bin`)** — Independent Lean 4 kernel implemented in Rust, which accepted Sutherland's 61.57 GB export (`exit 0` in 27 min 42 s on 80 threads), Arledge's independently generated export (1,484,729 declarations), Naskręcki's reading-project run (1,484,733 declarations), and Sutherland's combined `Robin1984` export (`exit 0` in 33 min 46 s on 64 threads). — `compilation/kernel check` — [Community] Accepted full export across multiple independent runs — [nanoda.log](https://math.mit.edu/~drew/grh/nanoda.log), [Infatoshi/rh-verification-receipts](https://github.com/Infatoshi/rh-verification-receipts), [nasqret/riemann-rev](https://github.com/nasqret/riemann-rev) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-21)
- **`con-leche` and `con-ron` (Joachim Breitner and Lean FRO contributors, `leanprover/con-leche`, `leanprover/con-ron`)** — Independently written Lean 4 export checker (`con-leche`) and its Rust port (`con-ron`, `master` `3f22699b`), whose `master` / `--compare` builds in `--verified` mode accepted 1,468,418 solution and 51,692 challenge declarations and verified all five challenge theorems against the solution (plus both `Robin1984` inequalities against `ArithChallenge.lean`), whereas the older binaries bundled with Lean `v4.36.0` declined with `exit 2` on one mutual reflexive inductive type (`PrincipalizationCertificate` / `OrderCertificate`). — `compilation/kernel check` — [Community] `con-leche` (`master` and `--compare`) and `con-ron --compare` accepted (`exit 0`, run by Teżewski and Sutherland); older `v4.36.0`-bundled binaries declined (`exit 2`, not a rejection) — [leanprover/con-leche](https://github.com/leanprover/con-leche), [leanprover/con-ron](https://github.com/leanprover/con-ron), [con-leche-compare.log](https://math.mit.edu/~drew/grh/con-leche-compare.log), [con-ron-compare.log](https://math.mit.edu/~drew/grh/con-ron-compare.log), [Zulip #642338492](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642338492) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-21)
- **`lean4lean` (Mario Carneiro, `digama0/lean4lean`)** — Lean-in-Lean typechecker (`67f63b0c`, built with Lean `v4.33.1`), which checked all 175,187 compiled `.olean` files module by module in Sutherland's run (175,182 under default settings and 5 `decide +kernel` modules after raising the deterministic fuel limit; the `v4.36.0`-bundled binary was inapplicable to the `v4.33` core library). — `compilation/kernel check` — [Community] Passed on all 175,187 `.olean` modules — [digama0/lean4lean](https://github.com/digama0/lean4lean), [lean4lean-raised-fuel.log](https://math.mit.edu/~drew/grh/lean4lean-raised-fuel.log), [Zulip #642325559](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642325559) (2026-11-20)
- **Additional export checkers (`con-agua`, `Pestka`, `Gruszka`, `nanoclo`, `tenet`)** — Five further independent Lean 4 export checkers (`leanprover/con-agua`, `jasisz/pestka`, `jasisz/gruszka`, `sgraf812/nanoclo`, `keithadler/tenet`) reported by Szymon Teżewski as accepting `solution.ndjson`. — `compilation/kernel check` — [Community] Reported accepted by Teżewski — [Zulip #642355040](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642355040), [Zulip #642395135](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642395135) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-21)

### Formalization projects (Masullo draft and related criteria)

- **Ryan McCorvie and contributors** (`mccorvie/riemann-masullo`) — Organised an agent-assisted community Lean 4 formalization of Masullo's *The Music of the Primes* in waves (~4,700 theorems and 820 definitions stated and ~3,950 theorems proved across ~1,360 merged PRs by ~30 contributors, with a `lake comparator` harness on Lean `v4.36.0`), completing most of Wave 1 (Appendices A, C, D and Chapters 2–4, 9), reporting in Wave 2 that the printed proof of Structure Theorem I (`Theorem 5.2.1.1`) does not work and that `§B.4` explicit-formula local normalizations need recomputation, and seeding Wave 3 (Chapters 10–12 and Chapter 13) as section issues with statements in progress while Chapter 13 does not yet state the main theorem in the manuscript. — `formalization` — [Community] Wave 1 mostly proved, Wave 2 active, and Wave 3 seeded as section issues (rechecked 2026-11-22 04:35 UTC); `Theorem 5.2.1.1` under test and Chapters 10–13 not yet proved [Open] — [mccorvie/riemann-masullo](https://github.com/mccorvie/riemann-masullo) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-22)
- **David Loeffler and contributors** (Prove2Me mission and Lean Zulip) — Opened the Lean Zulip coordination thread and Prove2Me mission to formalize Masullo's draft backwards from the goal, reporting by 22:18 UTC on 20 November a formal Lean proof that RH follows from Masullo's stated Theorem 12.5.1.1, and on 21 November answered Ishaan Dixit's Zulip question (`#642435200`) about Alpöge's intermediate declaration `KatoTwoW`, explaining that in Alpöge's analytic normalization the critical point sits at $s = 1$ (right of the centre $s = 1/2$), where the $L$-value cannot vanish, so no $L$-value hypothesis is needed (`#642442900`). — `formalization` · `review` — [Community] Goal-from-Theorem-12.5.1.1 implication reported formalized; `KatoTwoW` literature-strength question answered on Zulip — [Prove2Me mission](https://prove2.me/missions/Masullo%3A_The_Music_of_the_Primes_%E2%80%94_Riemann_Hypothesis_specialization), [Zulip V3 #642314625](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Formalising.20Masullo's.20proof.20of.20GRH/near/642314625), [Zulip V3 #642357563](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Formalising.20Masullo's.20proof.20of.20GRH/near/642357563), [Zulip V2 #642442900](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642442900) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-21)
- **David Loeffler** (Mathlib `PoissonSummation.lean`) — Formalized the classical continuous periodization coefficient identity and Poisson summation used as the source correspondence in §14.4.2, together with the power-decay and Schwartz Poisson tiers (`Analysis/Fourier/PoissonSummation.lean:119–226`), complex-quadratic Gaussian Poisson summation (`Gaussian/PoissonSummation.lean`), two-variable Jacobi theta inversion (`JacobiTheta/TwoVariable.lean`), and the even Hurwitz kernel bridge (`HurwitzZetaEven.lean`) read at R3 in §1.2.2.7. — `formalization` — [Lean, read] — [Pinned Mathlib](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Gaussian/PoissonSummation.lean#L38-L129)
- **Sébastien Gouëzel** (`GaussianIntegral.lean`, `Gaussian/FourierTransform.lean`), **Kim Morrison and Nicolò Cavalleri** (`ContinuousMap/Periodic.lean`, `ContinuousMap/Compact.lean`), **Johannes Hölzl, Sébastien Gouëzel and Yury Kudryashov** (`Measure/Lebesgue/Integral.lean`, `IntervalIntegral/Basic.lean`, `Integral/DominatedConvergence.lean`), **Moritz Doll** (`SchwartzSpace/Basic.lean`, `SchwartzSpace/Fourier.lean`), and **Heather Macbeth** (`InnerProductSpace/l2Space.lean`) — Credited authors of the pinned Mathlib complex Gaussian integral, principal-branch ODE argument, rectangular Cauchy contour Fourier transform, compact-open periodization, whole-line $L^1$ patching, Schwartz Fourier continuity, and Hilbert-basis expansion proof bodies read at R3 in §1.2.2.7. — `formalization` — [Lean, read] — [Pinned `GaussianIntegral.lean`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Gaussian/GaussianIntegral.lean#L159-L326)

- **Lee Crossley (`leecrossley`)** (`leecrossley/dirichlet-window-transfer` and `Li-Hongmin/amazing-riemann-hypothesis` PR #1) — Formalized in Lean 4 (`DelayedPole.lean`) that applying the three-term delayed-difference operator $-g(u) + 3g(u - \log 2) - 2g(u - 2\log 2)$ preserves the pole conditions of Theorem 3.6(1) (`ZeroFree F θ` from `WindowHypU`) via the Laplace multiplier $(1 - 2^{-s})(2\cdot 2^{-s} - 1)$, and contributed the short example after Theorem 3.6 in Chapter 3. — `formalization` · `this book` — [Community] Archived formalization published and book PR #1 merged on 25 November 2026 in `aa691cf` — [PR #1](https://github.com/Li-Hongmin/amazing-riemann-hypothesis/pull/1), [leecrossley/dirichlet-window-transfer](https://github.com/leecrossley/dirichlet-window-transfer), [doi:10.5281/zenodo.24410863](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24410863) (2026-11-21)

## Exposition and teaching material

- **Aapo Lipponen and the LUMI project** — [Community] Traced the local Bloch–Kato interfaces in the auxiliary-twist route of Frontier K §§1.3–1.8. [Pinned community report](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/aapolipponen/rh-lean-check-lumi/905943b8950179296a1ab29db5ebf03495e709f1/docs/reports/frontier-k-twist-and-frame.md)

- **Terence Tao** — Lecture notes giving a contour-selection proof for the truncated zeta explicit formula (Theorem 21) and a smoothing exercise (Exercise 22), discussed in §4.10.7. [Original notes](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2014/12/09/254a-notes-2-complex-analytic-multiplicative-number-theory/#vnmef)

- **Community and literature.** T. Tao's lecture notes on Linnik's theorem (254A, Notes 7, following Chapter 18 of Iwaniec–Kowalski) truncate the explicit formula at the fixed power $T=q^2$ of the modulus and keep a separate power-saving error next to $\exp(-c\log x/\log q)$; P. X. Gallagher's 1970 density paper is the source of that prime number theorem. They are the classical comparison for the far-range numerical lemma of §4.10.5. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies `num_main`, `num_alg` and their consumer `psi_relative_far_range`. The Mathlib libraries they use are credited under Mathlib below. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-4-4105-far-range-numerical-absorption)
- **Community and literature.** E. Landau's *Handbuch* (1909, §53, pp. 193–194) recovers $\psi(x)$ from the smoothed sum $\sum_{n\le x}\Lambda(n)\log(x/n)$ by a difference with step $\delta x$, bounding the step interval by $\log(1+\delta)\log(2x)(\delta x+1)$; T. Tao's 254A Notes 2 (2014) give the direct truncated explicit formula (Theorem 21) and a smoothed formula with absolutely convergent zero sum (Exercise 22). They are the classical comparison for the differenced explicit formula of §4.10.6. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies `differenced_explicit_formula`, its ten local lemmas in `PNTSharp`, `PNTSharpA` and `PNTSharpB`, and the call in `psi_relative_far_range`. The Mathlib libraries they use are credited under Mathlib below. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-4-4106-differenced-explicit-formula)

- **Community and literature.** J. S. Milne supplies the formula $n=ef$ for complete discretely valued fields, the layer count behind $\sum e_if_i=n$, completions as inverse limits and the quadratic rings of integers used in the examples of §18.3.9. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the four counting proofs `ballCard`, `denseSurj`, `kerProp` and `normCard` and their assembly `locDegOf_of`. The Mathlib libraries they use are credited under Mathlib below. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-1839-local-degree-count)

- **Community and literature.** The Stacks Project supplies the integral closedness of valuation rings, the Krull topology and the transitivity of Galois groups on primes over a normal domain, finite and infinite. J. S. Milne supplies the norm argument for transitivity, the Dedekind–Kummer factorization and Hensel's lemma used in the examples of §18.3.10, with local duality and reciprocity. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies `placeProp`, the integral-closure helpers and `exists_smul_eq_of_over`. The Mathlib libraries they use are credited under Mathlib below. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-18310-integral-closure-and-conjugacy)

- **Community and literature.** J. S. Milne supplies the Frobenius element of a finite Galois extension with its direct existence proof, its conjugation and towers, the cyclotomic Frobenius, the factorizations and cyclotomic rings of integers of the examples of §18.3.11 and the arithmetic normalization of local reciprocity. The Stacks Project supplies transitivity on primes and the surjection of decomposition groups onto residue automorphisms, finite and infinite. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies `exists_frob_over`, `exists_isFrob`, the inertia-coset and conjugation lemmas and their consumers `chebotarevDegreeOne'` and `pFrobEx`. The Mathlib libraries they use are credited under Mathlib below. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-18311-frobenius-elements)

- **Community and literature.** J. S. Milne supplies the Krull neighbourhood basis, the extension and restriction of automorphisms, the Galois correspondence with the rule $\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/\sigma M)=\sigma\operatorname{Gal}(\Omega/M)\sigma^{-1}$, the remark that an absolute Galois group is defined only up to inner automorphism, the conjugation rule for decomposition groups, the discriminant and Eisenstein criteria used in the examples of §18.3.12, and the triviality of conjugation on cohomology. The Stacks Project supplies the characterization of the Krull topology by continuity of the action. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies `exists_galContinuousMulEquiv`, `exists_gsIso`, the place maps, `isPlaceOver_transfer_fieldIso`, `exists_subfield_model` and `nineG_of_iso`. The Mathlib libraries they use are credited under Mathlib below. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-18312-galois-transport)

- **Community and literature.** The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the continuous degree-two pullback, local invariant transport and its use in the sequence. J. S. Milne supplies the Kummer cup and Hilbert-symbol norm criterion used for the numerical traces. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-18313-local-invariant-transport)

- **Community and literature.** J. S. Milne supplies the ring of integers $\mathbb Z[\omega]$ of $\mathbb Q(\omega)$, local unit norms in unramified extensions, the tame Hilbert symbol and Kummer cup-product reciprocity formulas, and the root-of-unity dependence when passing from $\mu_{\ell^r}$ to $\mathbb Z/\ell^r\mathbb Z$ used in the examples and remarks of §18.3.14. The Stacks Project supplies the classical commutative-algebra background on localization at primes, residue fields, discrete valuation rings and integral closures. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies `ring_equiv_transports_residue`, `isNormalised_transfer` and the normalization handoff in `ptConcl_of_iso_of`. The Mathlib libraries they use are credited under Mathlib below. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-18314-residue-and-normalisation-transport)

- **Community and literature.** The Stacks Project supplies the classical statements behind the reduced-fibre trace of §12.4.4: independence of characters and the splitting of a separable algebra over an algebraically closed field, integrality of traces over a normal domain, miracle flatness, finite projective modules and the Laurent-monomial cohomology of projective space. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the formal fibre-trace proofs. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-12-1244-reduced-fibre-trace)

- **Community and literature.** The Stacks Project supplies the classical statements behind the reduced auxiliary projection of §12.4.4: finite morphisms as affine proper ones, Artinian rings and their nilpotent radical, the Nullstellensatz, cotangent spaces of smooth algebras, reducedness under smooth maps and étale algebras over a field. Arthur Sard's 1942 theorem gives the measure-zero set of bad translations. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the formal projection and transport proofs. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-12-1244-reduced-auxiliary-projection)

- **Community and literature.** The Stacks Project supplies the classical statements behind the jet forms of §12.4.4: finitely many irreducible components of a Noetherian space, the dimension of local rings under specialization, the linear form separating a rational point from finitely many others, and the cotangent dimension at a smooth point; it also records the Serre-vanishing route that the archive does not take. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the formal jet construction. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-12-1244-forms-with-prescribed-jets)

- **Community and literature.** Arthur Sard's 1942 theorem, in its equal-dimension case, and Serre's GAGA, for the analytic topology of a separated variety, its algebraic charts onto locally closed subsets of affine space, and the comparison of local rings that makes smooth points manifold points, are the classical sources behind the Sard step of §12.4.4. The Stacks Project supplies étale coordinates on smooth schemes, the cotangent dimension at a smooth point, and the separability criterion and Frobenius factorization for curves used in the characteristic-$p$ example. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the formal Sard step. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-12-1244-the-sard-step)

- **Community and literature.** Tadasi Nakayama's 1951 note gives the local form of Nakayama's lemma. The Stacks Project supplies its global form, the idempotent generator of a finitely generated idempotent ideal, Hilbert's basis theorem, the finiteness of the minimal primes of a Noetherian ring and the affine-local description of morphisms locally of finite type: the classical statements behind the finiteness of transversal zeros in §12.4.4. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the formal proof. The Mathlib libraries it uses are credited under Mathlib below. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-12-1244-finiteness-of-transversal-zeros)

- **Community and literature.** Alpöge and Claude supply the two archive proofs of projective avoidance and detection of opens by complex points. Oliver Nash, Devon Tuma, Johan Commelin and Kenny Lau supply the cited Mathlib developments on subspace unions, Jacobson rings, the topology of the prime spectrum and algebraically closed fields. The Stacks Project supplies the classical Nullstellensatz and Jacobson scheme references. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-12-1244-finite-avoidance-and-complex-point-detection)

- **Community and literature.** J. S. Milne supplies the classical isomorphism of decomposition groups with local Galois groups, the conjugation invariance of localization maps and the local class field theory used in the examples of §18.3.8. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the formal comparison `decEquiv` and the cohomology transport lemmas. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-1838-completion-comparison)

- **Community and literature.** John Tate supplies the local Euler and exact-annihilator theorems. J. S. Milne supplies the inertia filtration proof and the equivariant calculation at the residue prime. The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the formal local Euler construction and its applications. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-1837-local-euler-characteristic)

- **Aapo Lipponen (editor)** (`aapolipponen/rh-lean-check-lumi/docs`) — Published a ten-part reader's guide (`01-what-is-proved.md` through `10-open-frontier.md`), glossary, and seven AI-agent module reports mapping the analytic endgame, the $\sqrt{X}$ theta-lift chain, Galois representations, the `VWin` Tate–Shafarevich bound, and the ~250 named literature theorems, with explicit markers for unread lower layers. — `exposition` — [Community] AI-assisted module guide, publicly available — [rh-lean-check-lumi/docs](https://github.com/aapolipponen/rh-lean-check-lumi/tree/main/docs) (2026-11-20)
- **LUMI review agents, `frontier-f-divisibility` report** (`aapolipponen/rh-lean-check-lumi/docs/04-chain-and-one-half.md`, section "How the divisibility `Conclusion1` is proved") — Located where the inert-prime divisibility is proved, outlined the Gauss jet bound `kernel_a_one_generic`, reported a full reading of the gain identity `ea_A1U`/`ea_BrNum`, and outlined the chain accumulation `lift_bound_of_integrality`/`main_ind`, with an explicit list of unread steps. — `exposition` — [Community] AI-generated read-only report, not human-checked; Chapter 8, §8.4.5 reads these declarations line by line against the pinned archive — [docs/04](https://github.com/aapolipponen/rh-lean-check-lumi/blob/main/docs/04-chain-and-one-half.md) (commit `c6d0e2bc06`, 2026-11-21)

- **Marshall H. Stone** — Classical Stone–Weierstrass approximation theorem and its lattice formulation. **Alex Freire** — Lecture notes giving the two-cover real lattice argument. — [Stone](https://doi.org/10.2307/3029750) · [Freire](https://web.math.utk.edu/~afreire/teaching/m562s26/Stone_Weierstrass_proof.pdf)
- **Terence Tao** — 245A lecture notes (2010) and *An Introduction to Measure Theory* (GSM 126, 2011): Exercise 1.2.1 on density integration (cited in Mathlib's `WithDensity.lean:389`) and Exercise 1.2.21 ($m(T(E))=\lvert\det T\rvert m(E)$ by elementary row operations) as classical background for §15.4.3.2; Littlewood's three principles, the density of simple, step and continuous compactly supported functions in $L^1$ (Theorem 15) and Lusin's theorem (Theorem 18) as classical background for §14.4.2.3. **André Weil** (*Basic Number Theory*, 1967, Ch. I, §2) and **Nicolas Bourbaki** (*Integration II*, Ch. VII, §1) — Classical local-field modulus $\operatorname{mod}_{\mathbb C}(z)=z\bar z$, $\lvert\det\rvert$, and Haar-density transport cited in §15.4.3.2. — [Tao, 245A Notes 1](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2010/09/09/245A-notes-1-lebesgue-measure/) · [Tao, 245A Notes 2](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2010/09/19/245a-notes-2-the-lebesgue-integral/)
- **Paul R. Halmos** (*Measure Theory*, 1950, §§59–60), **Floris van Doorn** ("Formalized Haar Measure", ITP 2021, §6), and **Colin McQuillan** with **Sébastien Gouëzel** (MathOverflow question 456670, asked by Gouëzel on 2023-10-18 and answered by McQuillan on 2024-01-06) — The classical product proof of uniqueness of Haar measure, its first formalization with the record of the finiteness gap in Halmos §60, Theorem A, and the completion-regularity argument behind uniqueness without regularity, as followed by Mathlib and cited in §15.4.3.3. — `exposition` — [Community] — [van Doorn, ITP 2021](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ITP.2021.18) · [MathOverflow 456670](https://mathoverflow.net/questions/456670/) · [Halmos, GTM 18](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4684-9440-2)

- **Chapter 11 finite Tate cup and shifts.** [Community] Aapo Lipponen's LUMI window-bound guide and open-frontier notes document the broader proof route. [Window-bound guide](https://github.com/aapolipponen/rh-lean-check-lumi/blob/main/docs/reports/frontier-d-window.md) · [Open frontier](https://github.com/aapolipponen/rh-lean-check-lumi/blob/main/docs/10-open-frontier.md)

- **John Tate, Georges Poitou, J. S. Milne, Andrew Wiles, and the Stacks Project.** Tate and Poitou supply the classical local and global duality, Milne gives the invariant and cohomological conventions, and Wiles's Proposition 1.6 states the Selmer sequence and Greenberg–Wiles formula over the rationals. Stacks Lemma 9.10.5 supplies the algebraic-extension argument used in the cyclotomic section. [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-1835-selmer-exactness-and-greenbergwiles) · [Cyclotomic sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-18-18315-cyclotomic-and-infinite-place-transport)

- **Refine Technologies, Inc.** (`music-of-primes-report-by-refine`) — Published an eight-page AI-assisted audit of Masullo's draft (committed 2026-11-20 21:12 UTC; linked on Lean Zulip by Brent Yee at 22:38 UTC) listing ten gaps or errors, including the earliest written public report we found that the uniform bound on individual biorthogonal pairs at p. 451 (after (10.3.1.18)) fails because of clustered critical-line zeros, the Chapter 11 prime-shell truncation issue, and the omitted steps in Section 12.3 and Chapter 13. — `errata/gap` · `review` — [Community] AI-assisted audit report; p. 451 biorthogonal-bound failure independently reproduced by others and in "The story so far" (`chapters/02-the-story-so-far.md`) — [music-of-primes-report-by-refine](https://github.com/Refine-Technologies-Inc/music-of-primes-report-by-refine), [report.pdf](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Refine-Technologies-Inc/music-of-primes-report-by-refine/main/report.pdf), [Zulip V3 #642361492](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Formalising.20Masullo's.20proof.20of.20GRH/near/642361492) (2026-11-20 21:12 UTC)

## Commentary

- **Terence Tao** — Wrote on his blog that Alpöge, "using an internal AI model, has generated an (enormous) Lean-only proof of the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis", pointed to the Lean Zulip thread to follow verification, noted that "the digestion process will take some time", and observed in comments that Alpöge's route appears to combine automorphic, algebraic and arithmetic-geometric inputs while Masullo's draft sits "closer to Deninger's strategy towards RH than Connes'". — `commentary` — [Community] First-hand preliminary expert commentary — [What's New blog post](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2026/11/20/unforced-euler-singularity-unforced-navier-stokes-singularity-and-the-generalized-riemann-hypothesis/) (2026-11-20)
- **Kevin Buzzard** — Published an earlier Xena essay (12 November) on statement verification in Lean, moderated the Lean Zulip threads, noted on 20 November that exploiting a soundness bug would require bugs across several independently written kernels and that "we still await a successful `comparator` run", objected on the Masullo thread to publicly disassembling an unrequested draft, and asked on 21 November for clarity on whether full Comparator checks had finished. — `commentary` · `review` — [Community] First-hand Lean moderation and commentary — [Xena blog](https://xenaproject.wordpress.com/2026/11/12/and-the-proof-is-checked-in-lean/) (2026-11-12), [Zulip V2 #642235897](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642235897), [Zulip V2 #642306912](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642306912), [Zulip V3 #642326773](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Formalising.20Masullo's.20proof.20of.20GRH/near/642326773), [Zulip V2 #642446185](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642446185) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-21)
- **Ravi Vakil** — Released and hosted Masullo's PDF on his blog (writing initially that Brian Conrad had examined it closely for months, that it was "so far looking good", and that it involved no AI), and updated the post about eight hours later to note AI-assisted error reports from readers and add "(I make no claim as to the correctness of the proof strategy.)". — `commentary` — [Community] First-hand release post and update — [Math 216 blog post](https://math216.wordpress.com/2026/11/20/hurried-announcement-alessandro-masullo-grh/) (2026-11-20; updated 2026-11-21 02:54 UTC)
- **Brian Conrad** (quoted in *Scientific American*) — Stated regarding Masullo's draft, on which he had given feedback for months, that "To judge the success of the method requires the paper to reach a stage of more final revision" and "It's undergoing major revision." — `commentary` · `review` — [Community] Quoted statement in press — [Scientific American](https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/dueling-claims-of-riemann-hypothesis-proofs-emerge/) (2026-11-20)
- **James Maynard** (quoted in *Nature*) — Remarked on Alpöge's announcement: "If this fully checks out, it is clearly a historic milestone in mathematics." — `commentary` — [Community] Quoted statement in press — [Nature news](https://www.nature.com/articles/d41586-026-03812-7) (2026-11-20)
- **Andrew Granville** (quoted in *Nature*) — Criticised the form of Alpöge's release, stating "What he released is garbled" and arguing that a clearer exposition should have been prepared before announcing. — `commentary` — [Community] Quoted statement in press — [Nature news](https://www.nature.com/articles/d41586-026-03812-7) (2026-11-20)
- **Brian Conrey** (quoted by AFP via *France 24*) — Called Alpöge's announcement "a monumental moment in human history" while stating that he had seen only the remarks on Alpöge's website and had requested a manuscript. — `commentary` — [Community] Quoted statement in press — [AFP / France 24](https://www.france24.com/en/live-news/20261120-anthropic-researcher-says-claude-solved-math-s-greatest-riddle-1) (2026-11-20)
- **Martin Bridson** (Clay Mathematics Institute, quoted by AP via *The Independent*) — Called Alpöge's release "an announcement of historic significance" while cautioning that "the journey from today's announcement to a place of real understanding will be long and difficult." — `commentary` — [Community] Quoted official statement in press — [AP / The Independent](https://www.the-independent.com/news/claude-anthropic-san-francisco-rutgers-university-wall-street-b3089058.html) (2026-11-20/21)
- **Maksym Radziwiłł** (quoted in *Quanta Magazine*) — Commented on the absence of human explanation in a Lean-only release, stating that the object of mathematics is the question "why?" and "this doesn't answer it at all." — `commentary` — [Community] Quoted statement in press — [Quanta Magazine](https://www.quantamagazine.org/updates/transformation/) (2026-11-20)
- **Michael Harris** — Published an essay on his Substack stating that "very little sense can be extracted from the sketch in Alpöge's four-page announcement" and comparing the 17-million-line archive to his earlier prediction of a million-page computer proof. — `commentary` — [Community] First-hand essay — [Silicon Reckoner](https://siliconreckoner.substack.com/p/mathematics-as-gift-economy) (2026-11-20)
- **Joachim Breitner and Sebastian Ullrich** (Lean Zulip) — Explained on Lean Zulip what `con-leche --compare` and `lake comparator --paranoid` each check, noting that `con-leche --compare` acts as an independently verified statement-and-kernel comparator and that checkers with independent bignum implementations had already accepted the export. — `commentary` — [Community] Technical commentary by Lean / `con-leche` developers — [Zulip #642342529](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642342529), [Zulip #642449544](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642449544), [Zulip #642450248](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642450248) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-21)
- **Jonas Whidden** (Lean Zulip) — Posted a structural reading arguing that the novel step in Alpöge's proof is using a chain of about $\sqrt{X}$ theta lifts as a number-field substitute for Stepanov's high-order vanishing multiplicity, built atop classical Weil–Howe theta correspondence and Selmer machinery. — `commentary` — [Community] First-hand architectural commentary — [Zulip #642380994](https://leanprover.zulipchat.com/#narrow/channel/113488-general/topic/Discussion.3A.20Alp.C3.B6ge.20Proves.20the.20Riemann.20Hypothesis/near/642380994) (2026-11-21)
- **Scott Aaronson** — Wrote on *Shtetl-Optimized* that "my bet is that the proof will stand" while observing that "Mathematicians of course won't be satisfied with 17 million lines of Lean." — `commentary` — [Community] First-hand blog post — [scottaaronson.blog/?p=10344](https://scottaaronson.blog/?p=10344) (2026-11-20)
- **Sam Hopkins, Will Sawin, Timothy Chow and Stanley Yao Xiao** (MathOverflow) — Opened and answered a MathOverflow thread on how mathematicians can collaboratively digest and human-verify an enormous AI-generated formal proof. — `commentary` — [Community] Public forum discussion — [MathOverflow #518033](https://mathoverflow.net/questions/518033) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-21)
- **Steven Strogatz, James McKee and Alex Kontorovich** (quoted in press) — Offered early reactions in the press noting conditional consequences of GRH (McKee in *New Scientist*), context alongside recent PDE claims (Kontorovich in AP), and caution that independent human verification remained ahead (Strogatz in *Business Insider*). — `commentary` — [Community] Quoted statements in press — [New Scientist](https://www.newscientist.com/article/2600134-anthropic-mathematician-claims-ai-assisted-proof-of-the-riemann-hypothesis/), [Business Insider](https://www.businessinsider.com/reimann-hypothesis-anthropic-claude-solved-biggest-problem-in-math-2026-11), [AP / The Independent](https://www.the-independent.com/news/claude-anthropic-san-francisco-rutgers-university-wall-street-b3089058.html) (2026-11-20 to 2026-11-21)

## Mathlib

- For Chapter 3's endpoint-to-logarithm chain, the pinned headers credit **Zhouhang Zhou and Yury Kudryashov** for filter integrability and the Bochner FTC, **Yury Kudryashov, Patrick Massot and Sébastien Gouëzel** for interval integration and its FTC, **Ian Jauslin, Alex Kontorovich and Oliver Nash** for primitives on complex disks, and **Yury Kudryashov** for the Cauchy integral file. [Source record](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-3-local-integrability-at-a-filter)

- The file headers for §1.2.2.14 credit **Anne Baanen, Kexing Ying and Eric Wieser** for quadratic forms, **Andreas Swerdlow and Kexing Ying** for bilinear orthogonality, **Johan Commelin** for algebraicity and integral multiples, **Xavier Roblot** for integer lattices and covolume, **Joseph Myers, Sébastien Gouëzel and Heather Macbeth** for Euclidean inner products, and **Sébastien Gouëzel and Jireh Loreaux** for the finite normed product. **Andrew V. Sutherland** provides the classical lattice and volume reference. [Pinned sources and references](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-1-12214-trace-realization)

- For the supplies used in §1.2.2.13, the pinned Mathlib file headers credit **Judith Ludwig and Christian Merten** for algebraic lattices, **Ashvni Narayanan and Anne Baanen** for number fields, **Anne Baanen** for PID freeness and trace, **Alexander Bentkamp and Mohanad Ahmed** for positive matrix forms, and **Monica Omar** for the matrix-order file containing the zero-form criterion. [Source record](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-1-12213)


- **Anatole Dedecker** is credited in Mathlib’s normed-ring product-sum file. **Johannes Hölzl** is credited in the fiberwise and finite-distributivity files, and with **Edward van de Meent** in the extended summation file. [Normed-ring sums](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Ring/InfiniteSum.lean) · [Fiberwise sums](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/InfiniteSum/Constructions.lean) · [Finite distributivity](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Algebra/BigOperators/Ring/Finset.lean) · [Extended sums](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/InfiniteSum/ENNReal.lean)

- **Johannes Hölzl and Edward van de Meent** are credited in the header of Mathlib's extended nonnegative summation file. **Johannes Hölzl and Yury Kudryashov** are credited in its scalar order and coercion supply. **Terence Tao** gives the classical arbitrary-index formulation cited by the book. [InfiniteSum/ENNReal.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/InfiniteSum/ENNReal.lean) · [Data/ENNReal/Basic.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Data/ENNReal/Basic.lean) · [Tao's text](https://terrytao.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2012/12/gsm-126-tao5-measure-book.pdf)

- **Chris Hughes, Abhimanyu Pallavi Sudhir, Jean Lo, Calle Sönne, Sébastien Gouëzel, Rémy Degenne and David Loeffler; Mario Carneiro and Joseph Myers** — Mathlib’s real logarithm and real powers (`Real.log_le_sub_one_of_pos`, `Real.rpow_def_of_pos`, `Real.rpow_natCast`) and the bound $e<2.7182818286$ (`Real.exp_one_lt_d9`), through which `num_main` turns $y^{-\kappa/2}$ and $(M^{13})^C$ into exponentials of $\log M$ and gets $\log M\ge1$ for $M\ge3$ (Chapter 4, §4.10.5). Credited in the source headers. [Real powers](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Pow/Real.lean#L51-L62) · [Logarithm](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Log/Basic.lean#L307) · [Exponential bounds](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/ExponentialBounds.lean#L38)
- **Sébastien Gouëzel and Yury Kudryashov; David Loeffler and Michael Stoll; Michael Stoll; Bhavik Mehta; Chris Hughes, Abhimanyu Pallavi Sudhir, Jean Lo, Calle Sönne, Sébastien Gouëzel, Rémy Degenne and David Loeffler** — Mathlib's mean value inequality (`norm_image_sub_le_of_norm_deriv_le_segment'`), the pole-removed trivial $L$-function `LFunctionTrivChar₁` and the non-vanishing of $L(s,\chi)$ on $\operatorname{Re}s\ge1$ (`LFunction_ne_zero_of_one_le_re`), the bound $\lvert\chi(a)\rvert\le1$ (`DirichletCharacter.norm_le_one`), $\Lambda(n)\le\log n$ (`vonMangoldt_le_log`), and the derivative and modulus of complex powers (`Complex.hasStrictDerivAt_cpow_const`, `Complex.mul_cpow_ofReal_nonneg`, `Complex.norm_cpow_eq_rpow_re_of_pos`), through which `differenced_explicit_formula` bounds the kernel defect, the step interval and the removed pole (Chapter 4, §4.10.6). Credited in the source headers. [Mean value](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/MeanValue.lean#L326) · [Removed pole](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/NumberTheory/LSeries/DirichletContinuation.lean#L338) · [Non-vanishing](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/NumberTheory/LSeries/Nonvanishing.lean#L398) · [Character bound](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/NumberTheory/DirichletCharacter/Bounds.lean#L33) · [von Mangoldt](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/NumberTheory/ArithmeticFunction/VonMangoldt.lean#L151) · [Complex powers](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Pow/Deriv.lean#L190)

- **María Inés de Frutos-Fernández** — Mathlib’s spectral norm and the normed field `PadicAlgCl`, whose norm is defined from minimal polynomials, and the adic valuations of Dedekind domains; the archive’s closed-ball and power-kernel proofs rest on them (Chapter 18, §18.3.9). Credited in the source headers. [Spectral norm](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Unbundled/SpectralNorm.lean) · [`PadicAlgCl`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/NumberTheory/Padics/Complex.lean) · [Adic valuations](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/DedekindDomain/AdicValuation.lean)
- **Kenji Nakagawa, Anne Baanen and Filippo A. E. Nuccio** — Mathlib’s finiteness, freeness and rank of an integral closure in a finite separable extension, which give $|\mathcal O_K/p\mathcal O_K|=p^{[K:\mathbb Q_p]}$ in `ballCard` (Chapter 18, §18.3.9). Credited in the source header. [Pinned Mathlib file](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/DedekindDomain/IntegralClosure.lean)
- **Anne Baanen and Alex J. Best** — Mathlib’s absolute ideal norm; with the norm–inertia formula of the second file (Anne Baanen), it gives $|\mathcal O_F/w^e|=p^{ef}$ in `normCard` (Chapter 18, §18.3.9). Credited in the source headers. [Absolute norm](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Ideal/Norm/AbsNorm.lean) · [Norm and inertia degree](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/NumberTheory/RamificationInertia/Inertia.lean)
- **Thomas Browning** — Mathlib’s length-based ramification index and residue-field inertia degree, through which `kerProp` and `normCard` reach the exponents $e$ and $f$ of the local degree (Chapter 18, §18.3.9). Credited in the source headers. [Ramification index](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/RamificationInertia/Ramification.lean) · [Inertia degree](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/RamificationInertia/Inertia.lean)
- **Thomas Browning and Andrew Yang** — Mathlib’s invariant extensions of rings: a finite group acts transitively on the primes above a prime of the invariant ring (Thomas Browning), and the profinite version follows through finite quotients and a nonempty inverse limit (Andrew Yang). `exists_smul_eq_of_over` applies the profinite version to the algebraic integers (Chapter 18, §18.3.10). Credited in the source headers. [Finite case](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Invariant/Basic.lean) · [Profinite case](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Invariant/Profinite.lean)
- **Kenny Lau and Yakov Pechersky** — Mathlib’s criterion that an element is integral over the ring of integers of a valuation exactly when its valuation is at most one, through which `norm_le_one_of_integer` bounds algebraic integers in $\overline{\mathbb Q}_p$ (Chapter 18, §18.3.10). Credited in the source header. [Pinned Mathlib file](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Valuation/Integral.lean)
- **Nailin Guan, Yuyang Zhao, Jujian Zhang and Sebastian Monnet** — Mathlib’s infinite Galois theory, the profinite structure of Galois groups and the Krull topology with its open fixing subgroups, used by `isInvariant_intCl`, `isOpen_setOf_apply` and `exists_smul_eq_of_over` (Chapter 18, §18.3.10). Credited in the source headers. [Infinite Galois theory](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/FieldTheory/Galois/Infinite.lean) · [Profinite Galois groups](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/FieldTheory/Galois/Profinite.lean) · [Krull topology](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/FieldTheory/KrullTopology.lean)
- **Thomas Browning; Andrew Yang; Chris Hughes, Joey van Langen and Casper Putz; Anne Baanen and Yongle Hu** — Mathlib’s lifting of residue-field automorphisms, through which `exists_frob_over` produces Frobenius elements in the absolute Galois group (Chapter 18, §18.3.11): the surjection of the stabilizer of a prime onto the automorphisms of its residue extension for a finite group (`Invariant/Basic.lean`, Thomas Browning); its profinite version, and arithmetic Frobenius elements with their inertia and conjugation lemmas (`Invariant/Profinite.lean` and `Frobenius.lean`, Andrew Yang); the Frobenius automorphism of an algebraic extension of a finite field (`FieldTheory/Finite/Basic.lean`, Chris Hughes, Joey van Langen and Casper Putz); the stabilizer homomorphism and its inertia kernel (`Ideal/Over.lean`, Anne Baanen and Yongle Hu). Credited in the source headers. [Finite case](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Invariant/Basic.lean) · [Profinite case](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Invariant/Profinite.lean) · [Frobenius elements](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Frobenius.lean) · [Finite fields](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/FieldTheory/Finite/Basic.lean) · [Stabilizer homomorphism](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Ideal/Over.lean)

- **Johan Commelin; Chris Hughes, Joey van Langen and Casper Putz; María Inés de Frutos-Fernández; Anatole Dedecker; Kevin Buzzard and Xavier Roblot** — Mathlib's Henselian local rings and the Henselian property of adically complete rings (`HenselianLocalRing`, `IsAdicComplete.henselianRing`), the finite-field counting lemma `FiniteField.exists_root_sum_quadratic`, adic completions with `FiniteAdeleRing` and `Ideal.finite_factors`, restricted products (`RestrictedProduct`), and the maximal real subfield with the CM conjugation (`maximalRealSubfield`, `NumberField.IsCMField.complexConj`), on which the archive's `exists_adelic_neg_one` rests (Chapter 6, §6.4.17). Credited in the source headers. [Pinned `Henselian.lean`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Henselian.lean) · [Pinned `FiniteAdeleRing.lean`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/DedekindDomain/FiniteAdeleRing.lean)
- **Sebastian Monnet; Thomas Browning and Nailin Guan; Kenny Lau and Yury Kudryashov; Thomas Browning and Patrick Lutz; Kenny Lau** — Mathlib’s neighbourhood basis of the Krull topology, continuous multiplicative equivalences (`≃ₜ*`), `AlgEquiv.ofRingEquiv` and the automorphism group of an algebra, finite adjunction of algebraic elements, and algebraic closures, through which `exists_galContinuousMulEquiv` builds conjugation by $E$ as an isomorphism of topological groups (Chapter 18, §18.3.12). Credited in the source headers. [Krull topology](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/FieldTheory/KrullTopology.lean) · [Continuous isomorphisms](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/ContinuousMonoidHom.lean) · [Algebra equivalences](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Algebra/Algebra/Equiv.lean) · [Adjunction](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/FieldTheory/IntermediateField/Adjoin/Basic.lean) · [Algebraic closures](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/FieldTheory/IsAlgClosed/Basic.lean)
- **Kevin Buzzard and Patrick Massot; Johannes Hölzl, Mario Carneiro, Patrick Massot and Yury Kudryashov; Kexing Ying** — Mathlib’s quotient groups with `QuotientGroup.congr`, the quotient topology of a topological group and its quotient map, closures of subgroups, and normal closures, through which `map_mem_closedNormal` and `quotientIso` build the isomorphism $\Psi$ of the groups $G_{F,S}$ (Chapter 18, §18.3.12). Credited in the source headers. [Quotient groups](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/GroupTheory/QuotientGroup/Defs.lean) · [Topological quotient groups](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Group/Quotient.lean) · [Topological groups](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/Group/Basic.lean) · [Subgroups](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Algebra/Group/Subgroup/Basic.lean)

- **Yury Kudryashov (Mathlib)** — Authored the cited gauge and Bochner divergence developments. The former handles countable exceptions through local face estimates, and the latter supplies the planar theorem called by the Cauchy wrapper. [Gauge divergence](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/DivergenceTheorem.lean#L66-L279) · [Bochner divergence](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/DivergenceTheorem.lean#L108-L536)

- **Yury Kudryashov (Mathlib)** — Authored the cited Lean circle-integral and Cauchy-integral developments that supply the P5 differentiable-to-analytic bridge. [Circle-integral source](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/CircleIntegral.lean#L623-L746) · [Cauchy-integral source](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/CauchyIntegral.lean#L589-L651) The same development supplies annular invariance via the planar divergence theorem. [Divergence interface](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/DivergenceTheorem.lean#L503-L514)

- **Yury Kudryashov** — Mathlib’s McShane–Bochner comparison (`IntegrableOn.hasBoxIntegral`, with the indicator and null-function gauges), based on Gordon’s monograph, and the box-integral foundations with the Henstock–Sacks inequality, used for the area integral in the planar divergence proof (Chapter 3, §3.4.1); the Henstock–Sacks proof body (common completion of two prepartitions, integrability on subboxes, the $\pi_0$ form) is read in full in the Henstock–Sacks subsection, and Cousin's lemma with the subbox induction principle behind it (`Box/SubboxInduction.lean`, `Partition/SubboxInduction.lean`) in the Cousin subsection. [Comparison](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Integrability.lean#L37-L301) · [Henstock–Sacks](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Basic.lean#L578-L594) · [Cousin's lemma](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/SubboxInduction.lean#L94-L131)

- **James Sundstrom** introduced the relative oscillation lemmas and the oscillation/mass proof for bounded, almost-everywhere continuous box functions in Yury Kudryashov's box-integral framework. [Mathlib #13968](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/pull/13968)
- **Yury Kudryashov** developed the box-additive and GP divergence supply. **Ruben Van de Velde** ported the single-cut and signed-face constructors to Lean 4. [Mathlib #4054](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/pull/4054) · [Mathlib #4741](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/pull/4741)
- **Mathlib contributors** supplied the product-measure construction and finite-cover extensionality, ported by **Pol_tta** in [Mathlib #4433](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/pull/4433). **Sébastien Gouëzel** extended the product results to $s$-finite measures, and **Rémy Degenne** recorded the diagonal counterexample. The superlevel-set argument for arbitrary rectangle sides credits **Fubini** on MathOverflow. [Mathlib #8713](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/pull/8713) · [Mathlib #22033](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/pull/22033) · [MathOverflow answer](https://mathoverflow.net/a/254134/136589)
- **Jean Mawhin** developed the geometric irregularity and affine-error proof for the generalized multiple Perron integral. **David H. Fremlin** supplies the cited classical gauge and finite alignment accounts and the related Pfeffer estimate. [Mawhin](https://dml.cz/handle/10338.dmlcz/101777) · [Fremlin, Chapter 48](https://www1.essex.ac.uk/maths/people/Fremlin/chap48.pdf) · [Fremlin, §1D(a)](https://www1.essex.ac.uk/maths/people/Fremlin/n10805.pdf#page=3)

- **Yury Kudryashov and Mathlib contributors** formalized the split grid, the exact complement, the tagged-embedding filter map and the gauge equivalence used in Chapter 3. The Cauchy development credits **Johannes Hölzl and Mario Carneiro**. **D. H. Fremlin** gave the classical gauge-filter and Saks–Henstock arguments, including the Banach-target passage to a limit. [Split grid](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/Split.lean) · [Box filters](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/Filter.lean) · [Cauchy filters](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean) · [Fremlin's chapter](https://www1.essex.ac.uk/maths/people/fremlin/chap48.pdf) · [Fremlin's note](https://www1.essex.ac.uk/maths/people/fremlin/n10805.pdf)

- **Floris van Doorn** — Mathlib’s product measure and Bochner Fubini (`Measure.prod`, `measure_ae_null_of_prod_null`, `integrable_prod_iff`, `integral_prod`, `setIntegral_prod`), used at the planar theorem’s only Fubini step (Chapter 3, §3.4.1). Also the convolution file (`posConvolution`, `integral_posConvolution`, `Integrable.convolution_integrand`) beneath the Beta–Gamma identity (Chapter 2, §2.3.2.2). - **Oliver Nash** — Mathlib’s lemma that a vector space over an infinite field is not a finite union of proper subspaces (`Submodule.exists_forall_notMem_of_forall_ne_top`), the simultaneous-choice step of the archive’s jet forms (Chapter 12, §12.4.4). - **Jujian Zhang, Fangming Li and Joachim Breitner; Andrew Yang** — Mathlib’s coheight step (`Order.coheight_add_one_le`) and the finite decomposition of a closed subset of a Noetherian space into irreducible closed subsets (`NoetherianSpace.exists_finite_set_isClosed_irreducible`), the dimension count that confines the base locus of the jet forms (Chapter 12, §12.4.4). [KrullDimension.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Order/KrullDimension.lean) · [NoetherianSpace.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/NoetherianSpace.lean)

- **Andrew Yang; Amelia Livingston, Christian Merten and Jonas van der Schaaf** — Mathlib’s extensionality of points over an algebraically closed field (`ext_of_apply_closedPoint_eq`), affineness of basic opens of Proj (`Proj.isAffineOpen_basicOpen`), and closed immersions from sections (`isClosedImmersion_of_comp_eq_id`), used for the point lemmas of the jet forms (Chapter 12, §12.4.4). [AlgClosed/Basic.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/AlgClosed/Basic.lean) · [ClosedImmersion.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/AlgebraicGeometry/Morphisms/ClosedImmersion.lean)

- **Sébastien Gouëzel** — Mathlib’s equal-dimension Sard lemma (`addHaar_image_eq_zero_of_det_fderivWithin_eq_zero`, in the change-of-variables file, after Fremlin), which makes the bad translation parameters of the archive’s Sard step a null set (Chapter 12, §12.4.4). [Pinned Mathlib file](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/Jacobian.lean)

- **Johannes Hölzl and Mario Carneiro; Johannes Hölzl, Patrick Massot, Casper Putz and Anne Baanen** — Mathlib’s countable unions of null sets and the Lindelöf property of second countable spaces (`measure_biUnion_null_iff`, `TopologicalSpace.isOpen_iUnion_countable`), and the kernel criteria for singular matrices and endomorphisms (`Matrix.exists_mulVec_eq_zero_iff`, `LinearMap.det_eq_zero_iff_ker_ne_bot`), which pay for the union over charts and pass from the complex chart Jacobian to the real determinant (Chapter 12, §12.4.4). [Bases.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/Bases.lean) · [ToLinearEquiv.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Matrix/ToLinearEquiv.lean)

- **Christian Merten; Johan Commelin** — Mathlib’s étale coordinates for standard smooth algebras (`IsStandardSmoothOfRelativeDimension.exists_etale_mvPolynomial`) and finite type as a quotient of a polynomial ring (`Algebra.FiniteType.iff_quotient_mvPolynomial''`), which give the archive’s algebraic charts and the embedding of an affine piece in $\mathbb C^k$ (Chapter 12, §12.4.4). [StandardSmooth.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/RingHom/StandardSmooth.lean) · [FiniteType.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/FiniteType.lean)

- **Johan Commelin; Mario Carneiro and Kevin Buzzard; Thomas Browning** — Mathlib’s Nakayama lemma (`Submodule.exists_sub_one_mem_and_smul_eq_zero_of_fg_of_le_smul`), the idempotent generator of a finitely generated idempotent ideal (`Ideal.isIdempotentElem_iff_of_fg`) and the finiteness of the minimal primes over an ideal of a Noetherian ring (`Ideal.finite_minimalPrimes_of_isNoetherianRing`), which make the transversal zeros of the archive’s finiteness step finitely many (Chapter 12, §12.4.4). [Nakayama.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Finiteness/Nakayama.lean) · [IdempotentFG.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Ideal/IdempotentFG.lean) · [MinimalPrime/Noetherian.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Ideal/MinimalPrime/Noetherian.lean)

- **Mathlib contributors** — Mathlib's induction principle for monoid algebras (`MonoidAlgebra.induction_on`: cases `of`, `add`, `smul`), through which `aeval_gen_sub_one_surjective` proves that $X_q\mapsto[e_q]-1$ maps $O[X_q]$ onto the group ring $O[(\iota\to\mathbb Z/n)]$ (Chapter 10, §10.4.7.4). [Pinned source](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/Defs.lean)
- **Christian Merten; Kenny Lau, Chris Hughes and Mario Carneiro** — Mathlib's denominator-clearing lemma for spans over a localization (`multiple_mem_span_of_mem_localization_span`, Christian Merten), which turns $\operatorname{span}_KB=V$ into $\operatorname{span}_K(B\cap W)=W$ in the archive's `span_inf_of_span_top`, and the unit criterion of a local ring (`IsLocalRing.residue_ne_zero_iff_isUnit`, file by Kenny Lau, Chris Hughes and Mario Carneiro), through which the archive's `propE` gets unit left Euler factors (Chapter 10, §10.4.7.3). Credited in the source headers. [Localization/Finiteness.lean:96–118](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/Localization/Finiteness.lean#L96-L118) · [LocalRing/ResidueField/Basic.lean:41–42](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/RingTheory/LocalRing/ResidueField/Basic.lean#L41-L42)

- **Maksym Radziwill** — Authored the normalized Borel–Carathéodory theorem used in the local logarithmic derivative bound. — formalization · [Community] Pinned Mathlib source checked 2026-11-24. [Mathlib](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/BorelCaratheodory.lean#L1-L23)

- **Lawrence Wu** — Authored Mathlib’s Mellin inversion proof from Fourier inversion, used in the archive’s Perron formula. [Pinned source](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/MellinInversion.lean#L1-L17)

- **Yury Kudryashov; Vincent Beffara and Stefan Kebekus** — Mathlib's maximum modulus theorem on bounded domains (`Complex.norm_le_of_forall_mem_frontier_norm_le`), holomorphy of slopes (`differentiableOn_dslope`) and the analytic-order API (`analyticOrderNatAt`), used by statement in the disc and rectangle proofs of Chapter 4, §4.10.8. [AbsMax](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Complex/AbsMax.lean#L405) · [Order](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/Analytic/Order.lean#L61-L90)

- **Floris van Doorn** is credited in the Haar, content and finite-product files, and **Heather Macbeth** shares credit for the lower marginals. **Johannes Hölzl and Mario Carneiro** are credited in the outer-measure, induced measure and measure-space files. The group-topology suppliers credit **Johannes Hölzl, Mario Carneiro and Patrick Massot**. The compact-set supplier credits **Floris van Doorn and Yaël Dillies**. **Paul R. Halmos**, **Jonathan Gleason** and **Terence Tao** supply the classical covering, extension and product precedents. — [Lean, read] for the listed production bodies · [Community] for the classical sources.
