## Sources for the lattice estimates

- The pinned Alpöge archive supplies the inverse-torus bound, its production
  by rational coordinate enlargement and its use in the theta integral.
  NIST DLMF provides the Poisson identity for the elementary example and
  Gan–Qiu–Takeda record the classical convergence range.
  [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-19-1933-lattice-majorant)

- The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the three denominator and scaled-norm interfaces and the two compact-action bounds. Sutherland and Milne provide background on adelic topology and full ideal lattices. The 24 November same-model review of §19.3.3 checked the conditional estimate and identified these arithmetic inputs for follow-up. [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-19-1934-finite-support-and-minkowski-lattices)

- The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the weighted integral lattice count, its one-dimensional cell lemmas and the coordinate bound through the canonical embedding. Xavier Roblot (canonical-embedding lattice basis) and Ashvni Narayanan and Anne Baanen (integral basis) supply the Mathlib inputs [Community]. NIST DLMF and MathWorld supply the zeta and trigamma values, and Milne supplies the discriminant background. The 24 November reviews of §§19.3.4–19.3.5 listed this count as a named input for follow-up. [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-19-1937-2026-11-24)

- The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the CM-type place identification `mk_tauE_bijective`, its restriction `places_bijective`, the enumeration `factEmb` and the separate proof `mk_tauE_bij`. Xavier Roblot (Mathlib infinite places, CM fields and the canonical embedding; complex embeddings with Alex J. Best) supplies the place, conjugation and restriction lemmas. J. S. Milne and Andrew V. Sutherland provide the classical CM-type and Minkowski-space background. [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-19-1938-2026-11-24)

- The Alpöge–Claude archive supplies the host structures `HostRaw`, `HostDer` and `HostAx`, the general producer `HostRaw.der` with `Der.cmType`, `Der.conj_apply`, `Der.cm` and the degree lemmas, and the host-supply call sites. Xavier Roblot (Mathlib `CMField.lean`, `IsCMField.of_forall_isConj`; `maximalRealSubfield` in `TotallyRealComplex.lean`) and the Mathlib cyclotomic-extension authors (`galEquivZMod`) supply the CM-field criterion and the cyclotomic Galois group [Community]. J. S. Milne provides the classical CM-field criterion and the cyclotomic conjugation remark. [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-19-1939-2026-11-25)

**Chapter 8 `ea_RestSU` producers.** Levent Alpöge with Claude authored the logarithmic-coordinate integrality transfer and the exponential-factor reduction in A2 (`MultisetValuation/AtPiEqRescale.lean`, `EaRestSExpVOf.lean`, `InParamMap.lean`, `IntRPsubst.lean`, `SubstitutionOfVariables.lean`, `NatCastInvMemRp.lean`, `IntegralExpRename.lean`), and the named inputs `restBeta` and `restGc`. Antoine Chambert-Loir and María Inés de Frutos-Fernández (Mathlib `RingTheory/PowerSeries/Substitution.lean`, `RingTheory/MvPowerSeries/Substitution.lean`: `substInvOfIsUnit`, `rescale`, `subst_comp_subst_apply`, `map_subst`) and Johan Commelin and Kenny Lau (Mathlib `RingTheory/MvPowerSeries/Inverse.lean`: `invOfUnit`, `mul_invOfUnit`) supply the formal inverse-function, composition and inversion lemmas (file headers checked at the pinned commit on 2026-11-25); B. Dwork supplies the constant of valuation $1/(p-1)$ `[Dwork1960]` and M. Hazewinkel the functional-equation view of the logarithm `[Hazewinkel1978]`. Our contribution is the reading and the two hand calculations (§8.4.14).

**Chapter 8 biadditive supply `restBeta`.** Levent Alpöge with Claude authored the translation of `BeProp` into `BetaHyp`, the Teichmüller-root and conjugation lemmas, and the application of `betaIsG` over $\mathrm{Frac}\,W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p)$ in A2 (`MultisetValuation/BiadditiveSecondVariable.lean`, `BiadditiveFirstVariable.lean`, `IntegralExpParamOfEquivZero.lean`, `PositivityConditionHolds.lean`), together with the named inputs `betaIsG`, `lawExists`, `witt_teichmuller_root` and the `WModel` norm. Johan Commelin (Mathlib `RingTheory/WittVector/Teichmuller.lean`, `Frobenius.lean`), Michael Stoll (`RingTheory/RootsOfUnity/EnoughRootsOfUnity.lean`), Kenny Lau, Mario Carneiro, Johan Commelin, Amelia Livingston and Anne Baanen (`RingTheory/Localization/`), and Antoine Chambert-Loir and María Inés de Frutos Fernández (`RingTheory/MvPowerSeries/Substitution.lean`) supply the Teichmüller, roots-of-unity, localization and substitution lemmas (file headers checked at the pinned commit on 2026-11-25); B. Dwork supplies the exponential integrality criterion `[Dwork1960]` and J.-P. Serre the Witt-vector background `[SerreLocalFields]`. Our contribution is the reading, the two hand calculations and the consumer map (§8.4.16).

**Chapter 8 conditional assembly of `BetaIsG`.** Levent Alpöge with Claude authored `BetaIsG_of`, `StepII_of`, `StepH_of`, `c_add_sigma_c_eq_zero`, `StepIV_of`, `g_zero_relation_holds`, `identity_b`, `frobenius_expression_g`, `swap_rules`, and `frob_congr` in A2 (`UniformThetaSubstitution/FinalS.lean`, `FrobeniusLiftNorms/NormLiftLeOne.lean`, `PartialCoboundaryLinear.lean`, `ConstantCoeffFirstTwoOfThree.lean`, `TopsChoicePartA.lean`, `ScalingSwapLemmas.lean`, `LocalComponentGL.lean`, `LogarithmEquivalence.lean`, `FormalGroupLogProofs/ChainRule.lean`), together with the named upstream supplies `DworkMv`, `DivDeriv`, `DeIntegral`, `LawSymm`, `LemmaOne`/`LemmaTwo`, `StepZero`, `StepI`, and `SimeqRules`. Justus Springer (`Mathlib/RingTheory/MvPowerSeries/Derivative.lean`), Wenrong Zou (`Mathlib/RingTheory/MvPowerSeries/Expand.lean`), Richard M. Hill and Ralf Stephan (`Mathlib/RingTheory/PowerSeries/Derivative.lean`), Antoine Chambert-Loir and María Inés de Frutos Fernández (`Mathlib/RingTheory/MvPowerSeries/Substitution.lean`), and Yakov Pechersky and David Loeffler (`Mathlib/Analysis/Normed/Group/Ultra.lean`) authored the pinned Mathlib formal-derivative, power-expansion, substitution, and ultrametric-norm lemmas (headers checked on 2026-11-25); B. Dwork `[Dwork1958]`, `[Dwork1962]`, J.-P. Serre `[Serre1960]`, J. Dieudonné `[Dieudonne1957]`, T. Honda `[Honda1970]`, and M. Hazewinkel `[Hazewinkel1978]` supply the classical exponential-integrality and formal-group context. Our contribution is the conditional textbook proof, the §8.4.16 Example 2 audit repair, and the two all-orders hand calculations (§8.4.17).
**Chapter 7 provenance.** Alpöge and Claude supply the archive’s rank-descent and full-rank germ argument. Mínguez and Satake supply the classical comparisons. The independent LUMI guide records the chain’s rank and parameter increments. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-7-7431--spherical-split-place-transfer)

**Chapter 7 single-cell provenance.** Bernstein and Zelevinsky supply the classical orbit integral. Alpöge and Claude supply the archive’s stabilized finite averages, normalized model transports and unit evaluation. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-7-7432--single-cell-evaluation)

**Chapter 7 filtration provenance.** Bernstein and Zelevinsky supply the classical orbit filtration and its proof through exactness; Zelevinsky supplies the $\mathrm{GL}_n$ form indexed by matrices. Alpöge and Claude supply the archive's rank-array order of the double cosets, the image filtration, the descent of the cell map to coinvariant classes and the assembly. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-7-7434--bruhat-filtration)

**Chapter 7 level-supply provenance.** Bernstein and Zelevinsky supply the notion of a limit of compact subgroups and the Baire category argument for orbit maps. Alpöge and Claude supply the archive's entry bounds, the levels normalized by the integral Levi, the compact support fibre and the cell-support level; the openness of the orbit map comes from Mathlib's open mapping theorem. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-7-7435--levels-and-support-fibres)

**Chapter 7 cell-constituent provenance.** Bernstein and Zelevinsky supply the classical exactness, exterior-tensor classification and finite-length preservation theorems, and Zelevinsky supplies the matrix-indexed coordinate gluing and graded bialgebra formulation for $\mathrm{GL}_n$. Alpöge and Claude supply the archive's $\mathbb N_\infty$-valued extended multiplicity and its unconditional exact additivity, factorwise exterior-tensor exactness, flattened-to-nested block swapping, column-induction exactness via finite $(P_{\mathrm{col}}\cap J)$-orbit averaging, the multilinear constituent expansion and `CellSemis.cellSemisimplification`. Nailin Guan, Yi Song, Xuchun Li and Bryan Wang authored Mathlib's `ProfiniteGrp.exist_openNormalSubgroup_sub_open_nhds_of_one` (`Mathlib/Topology/Algebra/ClopenNhdofOne.lean:48` at pin `0df444a3`) [Community]. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-7-7436--cell-constituents-and-extended-multiplicities)

**Chapter 7 exterior-tensor classification provenance.** Bernstein and Zelevinsky (1976, 1977), Zelevinsky (1980), Flath (1979) and Casselman supply the classical admissible Schur lemma and exterior-tensor classification over locally profinite product groups, and Jacobson (1945) supplies the density theorem for simple modules. Alpöge and Claude supply the archive's Hecke-algebra-free proofs (`PiExteriorTensorSmoothIrreducibleIsIrreducible`, `ExistsEquivPiExteriorTensorBlockLevi`, `TensorProductDeterminesFactorClasses`, `SchurAdmissible`, `ParabolicInductionTensorProductHasFiniteLength`, `ExistsIsLocCompAndIsLocLoc`, `InjectiveTensorLiftEquivariantMapsOfIrreducible`, `ExistsEquivExteriorTensorOfIrreducibleProdRep`, `ExistsIrreducibleSmoothQuotient`, `ContractMultilinearApply`). Aaron Anderson, Alexander Bentkamp, Daniel Morrison, Sophie Morel and Junyan Xu authored the pinned Mathlib modules (`SimpleModule/Basic.lean`, `Eigenspace/Triangularizable.lean`, `PiTensorProduct/Basis.lean`, `SimpleModule/Rank.lean` at `0df444a3`) [Community]. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-7-7437--exterior-tensor-classification-on-a-block-levi)

### Mathlib analytic continuation

- Yury Kudryashov, David Loeffler, and Terence Tao contributed the Mathlib removable-singularity, Hurwitz/Gamma, and zeta-asymptotics formalizations cited in Chapter 1. The declaration-level attributions are in the [source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-1-1222--removable-values-and-reciprocal-gamma).

- **David Loeffler** — Mathlib's Gamma construction, Euler quotient, reflection and nonvanishing,
  archimedean factors, and formal sine product; also the Euler-integral convergence proofs, the integral
  recurrence and the exponential-decay integrability test `integrable_of_isBigO_exp_neg` used in §2.3.2.3. The sine-product file credits David Salwinski's
  cosine-integral method. [Gamma proof](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Gamma/Beta.lean#L228-L457) · [Sine proof and attribution](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Trigonometric/EulerSineProd.lean#L1-L20) · [Salwinski (2018)](https://doi.org/10.1080/07468342.2018.1419703)

- **Yury Kudryashov, Patrick Massot and Sébastien Gouëzel; Anatole Dedecker and Bhavik Mehta; Benjamin Davidson** — Mathlib's fundamental theorem of calculus file (the one-sided inequality for right derivatives after Rudin's Theorem 7.21, the fundamental theorem for integrable derivatives and the automatic integrability of nonnegative derivatives on a bounded interval), the improper-integral file (a.e. covers, monotone limits of truncated integrals along countably generated filters, the half-line criterion for nonnegative derivatives with its value, and the converse that an integrable derivative forces a limit) and the power-integrability lemma `intervalIntegrable_rpow'`, read in Chapter 2, §2.3.2.4 together with Loeffler's `exp_neg_integrableOn_Ioi`. Credited in the pinned file headers. — `formalization` — [FTC file](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/IntervalIntegral/FundThmCalculus.lean#L943-L1272) · [Improper integrals](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/IntegralEqImproper.lean#L89-L886) · [Power integrability](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Integrability/Basic.lean#L45-L67)
- **Sébastien Gouëzel** — Mathlib's Vitali–Carathéodory file (2021): the strict lower semicontinuous majorant and upper semicontinuous minorant of an integrable function on a $\sigma$-finite weakly regular space, built from open supersets and closed subsets of the steps of simple functions, a series over the dyadic increments and a positive integrable function, read in full in Chapter 2, §2.3.2.5 as the input of the one-sided fundamental theorem. The pinned headers list **Sébastien Gouëzel, Floris van Doorn and Yury Kudryashov** for `Measure/Regular.lean`, **Mario Carneiro and Johannes Hölzl** for `Function/SimpleFunc.lean` and the `Lebesgue/` lower-integral files, and **Sébastien Gouëzel, Antoine Chambert-Loir, Anatole Dedecker and Jireh Loreaux** for the semicontinuity files, whose statements are used there. Credited in the pinned file headers. — `formalization` — [Lean, read] for the Vitali–Carathéodory file — [VitaliCaratheodory.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Bochner/VitaliCaratheodory.lean#L92-L518) · [Regular.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Measure/Regular.lean#L308-L359)
- **Mario Carneiro and Johannes Hölzl; Rémy Degenne** — Mathlib's monotone convergence file `Lebesgue/Add.lean` (2018), with its measurable, almost-everywhere and limit forms and the reduction of Fatou's lemma to it, the decreasing form under one finite integral in `Lebesgue/Sub.lean`, the lower-integral definitions in `Lebesgue/Basic.lean`, and Degenne's construction `aeSeq` of measurable representatives for a sequence of almost-everywhere measurable functions (`Function/AEMeasurableSequence.lean`, 2021). These are read in Chapter 2, §2.3.2.6 as the monotone convergence step of the half-line criterion of §2.3.2.4 and, through the two-end truncation theorem there, of the signed improper limits at both ends of Euler's integral. Credited in the pinned file headers. — `formalization` — [Lean, read] for the cited declarations — [Lebesgue/Add.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Lebesgue/Add.lean#L33-L147) · [AEMeasurableSequence.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Function/AEMeasurableSequence.lean#L32-L117) · [Lebesgue/Sub.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Lebesgue/Sub.lean#L26-L146)
- **Zhouhang Zhou, Yury Kudryashov, Patrick Massot, Louis (Yiyang) Liu, Sébastien Gouëzel, Rémy Degenne, Mario Carneiro, Johannes Hölzl and David Loeffler** — Mathlib's Bochner dominated convergence wrappers (`Integral/DominatedConvergence.lean`, Zhou, Kudryashov, Massot, Liu), dominated finitely-additive set functions and continuous linear $L^1$ extension `L1.setToL1`/`setToFun` with sequential and countably-generated-filter dominated convergence (`Integral/FinMeasAdditive.lean`, `Integral/SetToL1.lean`, `Bochner/L1.lean`, `Bochner/Basic.lean`, Zhou, Kudryashov, Gouëzel, Degenne), almost-everywhere strong measurability of limits (`StronglyMeasurable/AEStronglyMeasurable.lean`, `StronglyMeasurable/Basic.lean`, Degenne, Gouëzel), finite-integral closure and $L^1$-norm dominated convergence (`L1Space/HasFiniteIntegral.lean`, `L1Space/Integrable.lean`, Zhou) through decreasing tail suprema (`Lebesgue/DominatedConvergence.lean`, Carneiro, Hölzl), and parametric differentiation under local uniform domination (`Calculus/ParametricIntegral.lean`, Massot; `Analysis/MellinTransform.lean` and `Gamma/Deriv.lean`, Loeffler), read in Chapter 2, §2.3.2.7. - **David Loeffler** — Mathlib's Bohr–Mollerup file (2023): the log-convexity of $\Gamma$ by Hölder's inequality on Euler's integral, the Bohr–Mollerup uniqueness theorem through the logarithmic Gauss quotients and two chord bounds, and Legendre's duplication formula for $s>0$, which `Beta.lean` extends to $\mathbb C$ and `Deligne.lean` rewrites as $\Gamma_{\mathbb R}(s)\Gamma_{\mathbb R}(s+1)=\Gamma_{\mathbb C}(s)$ (Chapter 1, §1.2.2.5). The pinned file headers list Rémy Degenne for `MeanInequalities.lean` (Hölder's inequality for Lebesgue integrals), Zhouhang Zhou, Yury Kudryashov, Sébastien Gouëzel and Rémy Degenne for `Bochner/Basic.lean` (its Bochner form), Alexander Bentkamp and François Dupuis for `Convex/Function.lean`, Yury Kudryashov and Malo Jaffré for `Convex/Slope.lean`, and Sébastien Gouëzel for `GaussianIntegral.lean` ($\Gamma(1/2)=\sqrt\pi$). The book compares the argument with Emil Artin's monograph (1931), which credits the method to Harald Bohr and Johannes Mollerup (1922) and proves log-convexity by Riemann sums where Mathlib uses Hölder. — `formalization` — [BohrMollerup.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Gamma/BohrMollerup.lean#L58-L452) · [Hölder](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Bochner/Basic.lean#L1144-L1209) · [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-1-1225--log-convexity-bohrmollerup-and-duplication)

- **David Loeffler** — Mathlib's abstract functional-equation pair and Hurwitz/Riemann functional-equation chain (`AbstractFuncEq.lean`, `HurwitzZetaEven.lean`, `HurwitzZeta.lean`, `RiemannZeta.lean`, `MellinTransform.lean`, `HurwitzZetaValues.lean`, `ZMod.lean`; with Michael Stoll on `DirichletContinuation.lean`), together with **Anatole Dedecker and Bhavik Mehta** for the power change-of-variables lemma `integral_comp_rpow_Ioi` in `IntegralEqImproper.lean` (Chapter 1, §1.2.2.6). The book compares the completed reflection and parity split with Bernhard Riemann's 1859 *Monatsberichte* memoir (citing Carl Gustav Jacob Jacobi, 1829), Adolf Hurwitz's 1882 functional equation, and Pierre Deligne's 1979 archimedean Gamma factors. — `formalization` — [AbstractFuncEq.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/NumberTheory/LSeries/AbstractFuncEq.lean#L80-L429) · [HurwitzZetaEven.lean](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/NumberTheory/LSeries/HurwitzZetaEven.lean#L254-L783) · [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-1-1226--from-the-theta-transformation-to-the-completed-hurwitz-functional-equation)

- **Hongmin Li (with AI assistance)** — Published *A Positive Reciprocal-Product Criterion for the Riemann Hypothesis* (`riemann_hypothesis_42` v3; explicitly not a proof of RH), proving that RH is equivalent to $T(x) \ll_\varepsilon x^{1/2+\varepsilon}$ for an entrywise nonnegative reciprocal-product kernel $P(y)$, establishing exact inertia $(1, m-2, 0)$ on squarefree primorial stars and unbounded negative inertia via Cauchy interlacing, recording the unconditional lower bound $T(x) \ne o(x^{1/2})$ (and that $T(x) = O(x^{1/2})$ implies RH with simple zeros), and certifying minimal nonnegative detectors of degrees 6, 9, 12, 15 and 18 removing the first one to five odd collective residues. — `proof` — [Paper] Published prior work (equivalence criterion and exact certificates); **relation to the November 2026 claims**: if RH holds, the bound $T(x) \ll_\varepsilon x^{1/2+\varepsilon}$ becomes an immediate consequence (**superseded** as a route to RH), whereas the explicit nonnegative kernel $P$, its primorial-star inertia bounds, the unconditional $T(x) \ne o(x^{1/2})$ statement, and the five residue-removing detector certificates **retain independent value and are unaffected** (and whether $T(x) = O(x^{1/2})$ holds, which requires simple zeros, is not settled by RH alone) — [rStation riemann_hypothesis_42 v3](https://rstation.ai/problems/riemann_hypothesis/papers/42) ([discussion post #91](https://rstation.ai/r/riemann_hypothesis/posts/91)), [Zenodo doi:10.5281/zenodo.24054950](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24054950) (v1 2026-10-23; v3 2026-10-29)
- **Hongmin Li (with AI assistance)** — Published *Cutoff-free positivity of localized Weil forms through the $(\log 11)/2$ threshold* (`riemann_hypothesis_45` v2; explicitly finite-window positivity and not a proof of RH), giving a cutoff-free infinite-dimensional Green-kernel response–Schur reduction and a reproducible 256-bit Arb interval-arithmetic certificate that the complete localized Weil operator satisfies $\inf \sigma(A_a) > 0$ in both parity sectors for all $0 < a \le (\log 11)/2$, together with the spectral-floor bracket $0 < \inf \sigma(A_{a_\ast}) \le 2.78 \times 10^{-38}$ at $a_\ast = (\log 11)/2$ (Remark 7.1). — `proof` — [Paper] Published prior work (computer-assisted certificate); **relation to the November 2026 claims**: if RH holds, Weil's criterion implies $\inf \sigma(A_a) \ge 0$ for every $a > 0$, so finite-window nonnegativity is **superseded** as a consequence of RH, whereas the cutoff-free response–Schur method, the RH-independent 256-bit Arb strict-positivity certificate, and the quantitative upper bound $\inf \sigma(A_{a_\ast}) \le 2.78 \times 10^{-38}$ in Remark 7.1 **retain independent value and are unaffected** — [rStation riemann_hypothesis_45 v2](https://rstation.ai/problems/riemann_hypothesis/papers/45) ([discussion post #89](https://rstation.ai/r/riemann_hypothesis/posts/89)), [Zenodo doi:10.5281/zenodo.24054983](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24054983) (v1 2026-10-23; v2 2026-11-09)
- **Hongmin Li (with AI assistance)** — Published *Beyond the positivity threshold of localized Weil heat semigroups: gauge rigidity, onset and sharp smoothing times* (`riemann_hypothesis_187` v2; explicitly unconditional operator analysis of $e^{-tA_a}$ making no claim on RH), proving that beyond de Andrade Silva's pointwise positivity threshold $2a = \log\rho$ ($\rho^3 = \rho + 1$), no measurable sign gauge or bounded positive density restores positivity of $e^{-tA_a}$ (certified by explicit negative heat pairings on $13/32 \le a \le 5/4$), establishing the sharp critical onset $C_\ast \varepsilon^2 + O(\varepsilon^3)$ of Beurling–Deny violation above threshold, and proving sharp $L^2 \to L^\infty$ ($t > 1/2$) and $L^1 \to L^\infty$ ($t > 1$) ultracontractive smoothing times with bounded continuous eigenfunctions (with v2 resolving the Corollary 6.1 trace-threshold factor-of-$2$ typo and clarifying the Dyson-series attribution in Theorem F, formally accepted 2026-11-22 08:55 CST / 2026-11-22 00:55 UTC). — `proof` — [Paper] Published work on localized Weil heat semigroups; **relation to the November 2026 claims**: uses zero information about the zeros of $\zeta(s)$; even if RH holds (so $\inf\sigma(A_a) \ge 0$ for all $a > 0$), failure of semigroup positivity, gauge non-Markovianity, the $C_\ast \varepsilon^2$ Beurling–Deny onset, and the sharp smoothing thresholds $t = 1/2$ and $t = 1$ are intrinsic properties of $e^{-tA_a}$ and **remain completely unaffected** — [rStation riemann_hypothesis_187 v2](https://rstation.ai/problems/riemann_hypothesis/papers/187) ([discussion post #104](https://rstation.ai/r/riemann_hypothesis/posts/104)) (v1–v2 accepted 2026-11-21 to 2026-11-22)
- **Hongmin Li (with AI assistance)** — Published *Littlewood-size zero clusters in Landau's local method: a Diamond–Montgomery–Vorhauer construction and the scale it costs* (`riemann_hypothesis_189` v3; explicitly an unconditional study of abstract extended Beurling zeta systems making no claim on $\zeta(s)$), constructing via rescaled Diamond–Montgomery–Vorhauer factors at $\gamma_k = \exp(4^k)$ extended Beurling zeta functions $F(s) = \exp(\int_{1-}^\infty x^{-s}\,dm(x))$ ($dm \ge 0$) that satisfy Broucke–Révész's local disk/growth hypotheses while carrying off-line zeros of multiplicity or simple-zero cluster size $\asymp \log\gamma_k / \log\log\gamma_k$ on $\sigma = b \in (1/2, 1)$, paired with matching distributional-positivity lower bounds on kernel dilation scales $a_k$ (with v2 recording the $|b - \sigma|$ horizontal shift in Appendix Proposition A.1(ii), and v3 adding Appendix A's sufficient summability conditions and contrasting divergence/phase-cancellation examples for product realization on a safe half-strip while explicitly citing the 20 November 2026 announcements and treating classical RH as open, accepted 2026-11-22 05:23 UTC). — `proof` — [Paper] Published work on optimality of Landau's local method on extended Beurling systems; **relation to the November 2026 claims**: operates on abstract measure-generated zeta systems ($dm \ge 0$, where off-line zeros genuinely exist) to establish method-level sharpness of local disk arguments, treats classical RH as open, claims nothing about $\zeta(s)$, and **retains full independent value unaffected** by whether classical RH or GRH holds — [rStation riemann_hypothesis_189 v3](https://rstation.ai/problems/riemann_hypothesis/papers/189) (v1–v3 accepted 2026-11-21 to 2026-11-22)

  account of the ambiguous class number formula, Ferrero–Washington, Sinnott). Our contribution is the
  exposition, the examples and the reading boundary. A search of the issues and pull requests of
  `leanprover-community/riemann-hypothesis` on 2026-11-24 found no community analysis of these declarations.

The $\mathbb Q(i)$ and $\mathbb Q(\zeta_8)$ calculations and the doubling remark are ours [Expository]; the formal proofs are Alpöge and Claude's. - **Chapter 19 host fields (§19.3.9).** We located the general producer `HostRaw.der` of the derived host properties, read its `cmType`, `conj_apply`, `conj_iff`, `cm`, degree and `mem_F` bodies, and proved in textbook form that the six fields used by §19.3 follow from the raw Galois, square-root and cyclotomic data, with the cyclotomic frame entering only through complex conjugation at $-1$. The consumer table reduces each use in §§19.3.6–19.3.8 to raw fields, and model instances over $\mathbb Q(i)$ and $\mathbb Q(\zeta_8)$ show that the sign character alone does not identify conjugation. This supersedes the [Open] status given to the field construction in §19.3.8; the raw-host constructors and the choice of $\varphi_0$ stay named inputs. ### Chapter 19 raw host construction (§19.3.10)

- **Community.** Levent Alpöge with Claude wrote the formal construction in archive A2. It comprises the character-group modulus `EvenGrp.QS`, the frame `ModData.frame`, the twisted Artin map `ModData.art` with `art_surj`, the record `CharData` with `toHostRaw`, the selection `exists_cmType`/`exists_hostNF`, the second constructor `D7.hostRaw` with the embedding lemma `layer_emb`, and the call in `fieldChi`. Michael Stoll (`MulChar.subgroupOrderIsoSubgroupMulChar`), Xavier Roblot (`galEquivZMod`), Thomas Browning, Patrick Lutz, Yongle Hu and Jingting Wang (`normalAutEquivQuotient`), Thomas Browning and Junyan Xu (`nonempty_algHom_of_adjoin_splits`), Peter Nelson (`Set.ncard`) and the Mathlib community supply the library results used at the pinned commit. Keith Conrad's notes on characters of finite abelian groups and on Gauss sums, and Milne's *Algebraic Number Theory*, supply the published background. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-19-19310-2026-11-25)
- **This book.** Hongmin Li (with AI assistance) traced the raw host consumed on the main path back to its producer, `AnyGrp.host` called by `fieldChi`, and the element $\varphi_0$ to `exists_cmType` in `SecondSetNoRelation.lean`. He wrote §19.3.10: Theorem 19.3.10, the conditional existence of the raw host under `HostHyp`, $\gcd(M,qd)=1$, $e\ge2$ and the splitting at the odd primes dividing $M$, with the order of the five choices. Lemmas 1–6 prove it in textbook form: the modulus, the frame with its cyclic kernel and the count $o=|\Xi|$, surjectivity of the twisted Artin map by the Chinese remainder theorem, the field as a fixed field in $\mathbb Q(\zeta_N)$ with `cycEmb` the inclusion, the square root as a signed Gauss sum, and the nonemptiness of the CM type. The subsection traces the wiring from `fieldChi` through `supplyOfAdmOf` and `hostOfFieldOf` to `exists_hostNF`, and works out two complete field-by-field calculations for $(q,d)=(59,23)$, $e=2$. These are the trivial group of modulus $1$ and the odd character modulo $3$; the second shows why the Artin map is twisted. A third example, modulus $59=q$, violates coprimality and has an Artin map of index $2$. Remarks cover which hypotheses the construction actually uses, the unused even branch, and the second constructor with its non-canonical embedding [Expository]. The formal proofs are Alpöge and Claude's. [Chapter 19, §19.3.10](chapters/19-published-layer-case-studies-ii.md#19310-producing-the-raw-host-from-a-character-group)

Theorem 19.3.11 states how one layer, one embedding and fixed roles of the three generators give all twenty-six raw-host fields, with the thirty `HostDer` fields split into read and named producers. - **Textbook.** Hongmin Li with OpenAI Codex assistance explains the cyclic block product, the simultaneous unit choice, trace descent and the lower determinant. The exposition supplies three explicit tensor calculations and follows the same scalar back through both place families. Its reading grade is local author-level R3 for the cited bodies. [§5.3.1.20](chapters/05-the-square-root-barrier-core-gm.md#53120-cancelling-a-power-in-a-local-tensor-norm)

### Chapter 5 odd-prime cyclotomic norm descent (§5.3.1.21)

- **Classical theorem.** Helmut Hasse's cyclic norm theorem includes the odd-prime case. Hasse credits Hilbert and Furtwängler for the prime-degree antecedent. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#odd-prime-cyclotomic-norm-descent-53121)
- **Formal proof.** Levent Alpöge with Claude supply the cyclotomic pushout, the transport of the original finite witnesses and the two tower norm equations in A2. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#odd-prime-cyclotomic-norm-descent-53121)
- **Mathlib community.** The pinned library contributes linear disjointness, completion maps, tensor equivalences and determinant norm identities, with file credits in the source ledger. [Sources](sources/SOURCES.md#odd-prime-cyclotomic-norm-descent-53121)
- **Textbook.** Hongmin Li with OpenAI Codex assistance explains the place square and the Bézout witness, and works out the real seventh-root cubic over the rationals and over the real quadratic base. The cited proof reading has author-level local R3. [§5.3.1.21](chapters/05-the-square-root-barrier-core-gm.md#53121-descending-an-odd-prime-degree-norm-after-adjoining-roots-of-unity)

### Chapter 14 absolute Cauchy products

- **Mathlib contributors** — Supply the normed-ring Cauchy product, the finite-fiber sum, the strict-radius norm estimate and the two continuous multiplication identities. The source ledger records the declaration history and the ports. [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-14-absolute-cauchy-products)

### Chapter 14 factorial ratios and the Banach exponential

- **Jean le Rond d'Alembert, Walter Rudin, and Ovidiu Costin** — Classical ratio comparison and eventual factorial decay at arbitrary arguments (d'Alembert 1768, Tome V, Mémoire XXXV, §I), $\limsup$ ratio/root comparison and entire complex exponential series (Rudin 1976, §§3.33–3.40, Ch. 8), and Banach-algebra exponential series and commuting addition law (Costin 2020, §§1.0–1.1). [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-14-factorial-ratios-and-the-banach-exponential)
- **Levent Alpöge (with Claude) and Mathlib contributors** — Edward Watine formalized `FormalMultilinearSeries.ofScalars` and `ofScalars_radius_eq_top_of_tendsto`; Anatole Dedecker, Eric Wieser, and Yuyang Zhao formalized `NormedSpace.expSeries_radius_eq_top` and its global Banach-algebra wrappers; Sébastien Gouëzel, Yury Kudryashov, Sophie Morel, Patrick Massot, Johannes Hölzl, Dylan MacKenzie, Heather Macbeth, and Jireh Loreaux wrote the multilinear-norm, convergence-radius, ratio-test, infinite-sum, and spectral files; Alpöge with Claude consumes these declarations in A2 (`NFRadRedR.lean`, `SphericalL1StatementOfProjectors.lean`, `JacConstBHLie.lean`, `LConv.lean`). [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-14-factorial-ratios-and-the-banach-exponential)
- **Hongmin Li (with AI assistance)** — Read the `OfScalars` to `Exponential` production chain at local R3, derived the logarithm-free uniform tail threshold $N_{\mathrm{arch}}(R,\varepsilon)$ and its supply to global exponential consumers, proved the exact operator-norm tail on the $\ell^2(\mathbb N_0)$ unilateral shift via coherent block vectors, worked out the non-normal $M_2(\mathbb C)$ remainder hierarchy in Euclidean and Frobenius norms, and constructed the nonvanishing and oscillating/finite-radius boundary controls. [Expository] [Chapter 14](chapters/14-masullos-trace-formula-route.md#factorial-ratios-and-the-infinite-radius-of-the-banach-exponential)

### Chapter 8 §8.4.31 — Control-class change and rescaled logarithm existence/uniqueness

- **Michel Lazard, Jonathan Lubin, John Tate, and Bernard Dwork** — Classical two-sided compositional inversion of zero-constant formal power series $uX + O(X^2)$ ($u \in R^\times$) over commutative rings (Lazard 1955, §I, p. 253, citing Bourbaki *Algèbre* Ch. IV §5), formal logarithms of Lubin–Tate endomorphisms (Lubin and Tate 1965, pp. 380–387), and critical $(p^2-1)$-st root scaling (Dwork 1962, §4(a), pp. 55–56). [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#ch08-control-class-change-log-rescale-sources)
- **Levent Alpöge (with Claude) and Mathlib contributors** — Antoine Chambert-Loir and María Inés de Frutos Fernández formalized `PowerSeries.substInv` / `substInvOfIsUnit` and substitution associativity in Mathlib; Wenrong Zou formalized `MvPowerSeries.map_iterateFrobenius_expand`; Alpöge with Claude proved `classChange_statement` (`TopsChoicePartB.lean`), `logRescaleUnique` and `logRescale` (`LogarithmEquivalence.lean`, `BallRing.lean`, `TopsChoicePartA.lean`), and their wiring into `ThmSCore_of` (`TheoremSFromKOneOf.lean`). [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#ch08-control-class-change-log-rescale-sources)
- **Hongmin Li (with AI assistance)** — Read the `ClassChange`, `LogRescaleUnique`, `LogRescale`, and `ThmSCore_of` proof bodies at author-level local R3, wrote the textbook exposition and same-object witness table, worked out the critical $p = 2$ (including the sharp degree-$16$ deviation) and $p = 3$ bivariate calculations, and proved the original-definition counter-controls on dropping $Y(0) = 0$, $\operatorname{Integral}(Y)$, nonzero/normalized $p$, and $\mu'(0) = 0$. [Chapter 8 §8.4.31](chapters/08-divisibility-at-inert-primes.md#8431-control-class-change-and-the-existence-and-uniqueness-of-the-rescaled-logarithm)

### Chapter 7 lower-derivative positivity

Bernstein established the strict lower-derivative spectral condition and the mirabolic pairing theorem. Bernstein and Zelevinsky supplied the derivative convention and filtration, and Zelevinsky supplied the irreducibility test used in the examples. Alpöge and Claude's A2 formal proof uses lattice averages, the atom-zero step and the derivative eigenvector on the original quotient. The textbook contributes the English reconstruction of the strictness argument, the two principal-series calculations, the hypothesis controls and the local source and consumer map at author-level R3. Existing project reviews of the highest, product and Jacquet results retain their own scope and are recorded separately from the source reading for [§7.4.3.20](./chapters/07-chains-of-theta-lifts.md#74320-from-positive-semidefinite-forms-to-the-strict-lower-derivative-bound). ### Chapter 15 §15.4.3.12 — Rational p-adic completion and discrete valuation

- **Andrew V. Sutherland, Robert Y. Lewis and Keith Conrad.** Sutherland's lecture notes give the classical Cauchy completion of a valued field, topological-field continuity, discrete valuation extension via eventual constancy and specialization to $\mathbb Q_p$. Lewis's CPP 2019 paper documents the Lean Cauchy-quotient construction of `ℚ_[p]` and its rational-valued stationary-tail norm. Conrad's notes supply the integer simple-root recursion and exact residual error bound. [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-15-rational-p-adic-completion)
- **Mathlib contributors and archive authors.** At the pinned commit, Robert Y. Lewis authored `PadicNumbers.lean` and `PadicNorm.lean` (with Matthew Robert Ballard on `PadicVal/Defs.lean` and `PadicVal/Basic.lean`, and Mario Carneiro on `CauSeq/Completion.lean`); Ballard and Yury Kudryashov authored `MaxPowDiv.lean`; María Inés de Frutos-Fernández and Fabrizio Barroero authored `IsNonarchimedean.lean`; Mario Carneiro authored `CauSeq/Basic.lean`; Yakov Pechersky authored `Padics/ValuativeRel.lean` and `Padics/WithVal.lean`; Jeremy Avigad, Lewis, Johannes Hölzl, Carneiro, Patrick Massot and Sébastien Gouëzel authored the metric/uniform sequential completeness files (with Gouëzel also authoring the named `Padics/ProperSpace.lean` supplier). Levent Alpöge with Claude authored A2's `GroundTheory/LocalFields/` bridge and local Fourier/reciprocity consumers. [Pinned Mathlib `PadicNumbers.lean`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/NumberTheory/Padics/PadicNumbers.lean)
- **Hongmin Li with AI assistance.** Wrote the textbook proof from rational divisibility and unequal-norm equality through stationary tail norms, quotient reciprocals, discrete value group $p^{\mathbb Z}$, rational density, diagonal completeness, and the pointwise comparison between A2's `ord`/`normalizedValuation` and Mathlib's `Padic.valuation`/`Padic.mulValuation`, together with the $3$-adic geometric/negative-order calculation, the $5$-adic simple-root calculation for $T^2-6$, and the composite-base $6$ counterexamples (`254` exact rational assertions PASS). [Chapter 15, §15.4.3.12](chapters/15-two-proofs-side-by-side.md#154312-completing-the-rationals-at-a-prime)


### Chapter 11 local finite-coefficient Tate dévissage (§11.4.6)

- **John Tate and J. S. Milne:** Tate (1962, Theorem 2.1) established local duality for arbitrary finite Galois modules of order prime to $\operatorname{char}(K)$, and Milne (*Arithmetic Duality Theorems*, 2nd ed., Ch. I §§1–2) gives the classical profinite induced-module dévissage and local specialization. **Jean-Pierre Serre** (*Local Fields*) supplies the local ramification facts used in the worked calculations. [Source ledger](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-11-local-finite-coefficient-tate-devissage)

- **Hongmin Li with AI assistance:** [§11.4.6](chapters/11-the-window-bound-vwin.md#1146-from-the-root-of-unity-base-to-arbitrary-finite-local-coefficients) reconstructs the five-stage dévissage proof arrow by arrow without five-lemma shortcuts, separates the roles of $n$-torsion and finiteness in contragredient exactness and discreteness, works out the unipotent $\mathbb F_3$-block over $\mathbb Q_7$ (verifying the minus sign in $x' - \delta_{S,0,1}(q_{\mathrm{corr}})$) and the ramified quadratic $\mathbb Z/4(\chi_{-3})$ twist over $\mathbb Q_3$ with nontrivial $\mu_4$, supplies four exact hypothesis-deletion controls, and maps the four archive consumers at author-level local R3.

### Chapter 2 §§2.3.2.7.2–3 — Riesz–Fischer and integral Minkowski

- **Classical sources.** Frigyes Riesz and Ernst Fischer supply the early scalar completeness results. Riesz’s 1910 memoir attributes the finite-sum inequality (2) to Hermann Minkowski and derives the integral inequality (6). Walter Rudin gives the modern scalar geometric-subsequence proof. The original completeness commit is credited to Degenne and records his co-authorship under a separate identity, with Sébastien Gouëzel credited alongside him in the current file header. Gouëzel introduced the nontrivial-target converse and the sharp low-exponent integral variant. Yongxi (Aaron) Lin introduced the filter bound on the size of an a.e. limit. Jeremy Tan Jie Rui split the completeness bodies into their current file. ### Chapter 12 inverse series, ordered composition and inverse charts

- **Classical source.** Jürgen Pöschel supplied the finite-cutoff inversion technique credited in Mathlib. [Theorem 5.2, p. 16](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1702.03691#page=16)
- **Mathlib community.** Sébastien Gouëzel introduced the inverse-series coefficient bounds in Mathlib 3 and the outer-sum recovery lemma in Mathlib 4. He introduced the composition estimates and associativity, and generalized forward composition within sets to the current normed-space hypotheses. Johan Commelin shares the composition file credit. Pol'tta / Miyahara Kō ported the inverse file and Jireh Loreaux ported the composition file. [Inverse bounds](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib/commit/c70feebd43e143d81a695f7d7e5b21e5892286e8) · [Recovery](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/commit/8d426f12d4550cf82ee62f169425f78684c746d1) · [Composition estimates](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib/commit/0bc15f82140e44f1a22c13aa5f819749612bddc5) · [Associativity](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib/commit/b1c0398ac610971e567889ca51e19db368c8777c) · [Within composition](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/commit/36b6e277cc8cf1014d2df084f3f91c0e4b9ea0d5) · [Inverse port](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/commit/cf365f06207ddb4eca360418f1bdc661200b137a) · [Composition port](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/commit/9b2617a8fca6427a635bd89ed9fa459f745f5aa0)
- **Mathlib community.** Yury G. Kudryashov introduced the general two-point filter cancellation theorem and its within, ordinary and strict forms. [Declaration introduction](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/commit/51113ff83eed9cfda06f94299e6dc82249091d2f)
- **Archive authors.** Levent Alpöge with Claude supply the parameter-preserving chart, the analytic zero slice and the chart-change application in A2. [Source and consumer table](sources/SOURCES.md#chapter-12--a-positive-radius-for-the-inverse-series)
- **This book.** Hongmin Li with AI assistance presents the four original textbook arguments as a continuous module. The explicit radius, examples, failure tests and differential formulas explain the pinned proofs and calls. The original majorant section has an independent audit. The ordered composition, forward composition and cancellation sections each have a scoped same-source initial review. [Chapter 12](chapters/12-galois-representations-and-local-langlands.md#a-positive-radius-for-the-inverse-series)
